such that, sampling non-intersecting Brownian bridges
\bm{y}=(y_{1},y_{2},\ldots,y_{n^{\prime}})
from
\mathfrak{Q}_{\mathfrak{f};\mathfrak{g}}^{\overline{\bm{u}};\overline{\bm{v}}}(n^{-1})
and denoting the associated height function by
\mathsf{H}^{\bm{y}}:\overline{\mathfrak{S}}\rightarrow\mathbb{Z}
, we have

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\Bigg[\displaystyle\sup_{z\in\mathfrak{S}}\big|n^{-1}\mathsf{H}^{\bm{y}}(z)-\mathfrak{H}(z)\big|\geq n^{\delta-1}\Bigg]\leq C_{2}e^{-c_{0}(\log n)^{2}}.

The following lemma bounds the distance between
\bm{Q}_{\varphi}(z)
and
\bm{Q}_{\mathfrak{H}}(z)
under this notation.

Lemma 6.2
.

There exists a constant
C=C(\varepsilon,B,m)>1
such that

(6.6)

\displaystyle\big|\bm{q}_{\varphi}^{(k)}(z)-\bm{q}_{\mathfrak{H}}^{(k)}(\rho,0)\big|\leq C(\rho^{m-k+1}+\omega),\qquad\text{for any $k\in\llbracket 1,m\rrbracket$}.

Proof.

The bound (
6.6
) holds at
k=1
by the first statement of (
6.4
) and the second statement of (
6.5
). So, we may assume in what follows that
k\in\llbracket 2,m\rrbracket
. To establish (
6.6
) in that case, it suffices to show that

(6.7)

\displaystyle\big|\bm{q}_{\varphi}^{(k)}(z)-\bm{q}_{H}^{(k)}(z)\big|\leq C\omega;\qquad\big|\bm{q}^{(k)}-\bm{q}_{\mathfrak{H}}^{(k)}(\rho,0)\big|\leq C\rho^{m-k+1};\qquad\big|\bm{q}_{H}^{(k)}(z)-\bm{q}^{(k)}\big|\leq C\omega.

To verify the first, observe from (
4.7
) and (
4.6
) that

(6.8)

\displaystyle\big|\bm{q}_{\varphi}^{(k)}(z)-\bm{q}_{H}^{(k)}(z)\big|\leq\omega\big|\bm{q}_{\psi}^{(k)}(z)\big|\leq(m+1)\zeta^{m}e^{\zeta|z|}\omega\leq C_{3}\omega,

for some constant
C_{3}=C_{3}(\varepsilon,B,m)>1
(where in the last inequality we implicitly used the fact that
|z|\leq 3B_{0}
, which holds since
|z|\leq\|f\|_{\mathcal{C}^{m+1}}+\|g\|_{\mathcal{C}^{m+1}}+L^{-1}\leq 3B_{0}
). The second follows from the last statement of (
6.5
).

To establish the third statement of (
6.7
), observe since
\bm{Q}_{H}^{(m)}(z)
and
\bm{Q}
are consistent that it suffices by
5.6
(and the first part of
6.1
) to show the existence of a constant
C_{4}=C_{4}(\varepsilon,B,m)>1
such that

(6.9)

\displaystyle\big|\partial_{x}^{k}H(z)-q_{0}^{(k)}\big|\leq C_{4}\omega;\qquad\big|\partial_{t}\partial_{x}^{k-1}H(z)-q_{1}^{(k)}\big|\leq C_{4}\omega,\qquad\text{for each $k\in\llbracket 1,m\rrbracket$}.

For
k\neq 2
, this follows from (
6.4
) and (
6.8
), and for
k=2
it follows from the fact that

\displaystyle\big|\bm{q}_{H}^{(2)}(z)-\bm{q}^{(2)}\big|=\Bigg|\displaystyle\frac{\bm{p}\big(\nabla\varphi(z)\big)\cdot\bm{q}_{H}^{(2)}(z)}{\bm{p}\big(\nabla\varphi(z)\big)\cdot\bm{q}_{\psi}^{(2)}(z)}\Bigg|\big|\bm{q}_{\psi}^{(2)}(z)\big|\leq\big|\bm{q}_{\psi}^{(2)}(z)\big|\frac{\omega\kappa}{2}\leq 2\zeta^{2}e^{3B_{0}\zeta}\omega\kappa,

where the first statement holds by (
6.4
), the second by the second part of
6.1
; and the third from the explicit form (
4.6
) for
\psi
, with the fact that
|z|\leq 3B_{0}
for
z\in\mathfrak{P}
. This confirms (
6.9
) and thus the lemma.
∎

In the below, for any point
w\in\mathbb{R}^{d}
and real number
r>0
, we denote the open disk
\mathcal{B}_{r}(w)=\big\{w^{\prime}\in\mathbb{R}^{d}:|w^{\prime}-w|\leq r\big\}
. We next have the following lemma, which is similar to
[
52
, Equation (88)]
, indicating the relative height of
\varphi(z)
with respect to
\varphi|_{\partial\mathcal{B}_{\rho}(z)}
is larger than that of
\mathfrak{H}(\rho,0)
with respect to that of
\mathfrak{H}|_{\partial\mathcal{B}_{\rho}(\rho,0)}
.

Lemma 6.3
.

There exists a constant
c=c(\varepsilon,B,m)>0
such that the following holds. Fix
w\in\mathfrak{P}
with
|z-w|\leq n^{1/2m^{2}}\rho
; set
z_{0}=(\rho,0)\in\mathfrak{S}
; and set
w_{0}=z_{0}-z+w
. If
w_{0}\in\mathfrak{S}
, then

\displaystyle\varphi(z)-\varphi(w)\geq\mathfrak{H}(z_{0})-\mathfrak{H}(w_{0})+c\omega|z-w|^{2}-c^{-1}\rho^{m}|z-w|.

Proof.

For any continuously twice-differentiable function
F
and point
z^{\prime}
in its domain, let the
2\times 2
matrix
\bm{H}_{F}(z^{\prime})
denote its Hessian at
z^{\prime}
, given by

\displaystyle\bm{H}_{F}(z^{\prime})=\left[\begin{array}[]{cc}\partial_{t}^{2}F(z^{\prime})&\partial_{t}\partial_{x}F(z^{\prime})\\
\partial_{t}\partial_{x}F(z^{\prime})&\partial_{x}^{2}F(z^{\prime})\end{array}\right].

Then, by (
6.4
), (
6.5
), a Taylor expansion, the fact that
w_{0}-z_{0}=w-z
, the first part of
6.1
, and
6.2
, there is a constant
C_{3}=C_{3}(\varepsilon,B,m)>1
such that

(6.10)

\displaystyle\begin{aligned} \Big|\varphi(w)-\varphi(z)-\big(&\mathfrak{H}(w_{0})-\mathfrak{H}(z_{0})\big)-(w-z)\cdot\big(\bm{H}_{\varphi}(z)-\bm{H}_{\mathfrak{H}}(z_{0})\big)(w-z)\Big|\\
&\leq|z-w|\cdot\big|\bm{q}_{\varphi}^{(1)}(z)-\bm{q}_{\mathfrak{H}}^{(1)}(z_{0})\big|+\displaystyle\sum_{k=3}^{m}|z-w|^{k}\big|\bm{q}_{\varphi}^{(k)}(z)-\bm{q}_{\mathfrak{H}}^{(k)}(z_{0})\big|\\
&\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad+|z-w|^{m+1}\big(\|\varphi\|_{\mathcal{C}^{m+1}(\overline{\mathfrak{S}})}+\|\mathfrak{H}\|_{\mathcal{C}^{m+1}(\overline{\mathfrak{S}})}\big)\\
&\leq C_{3}\rho^{m}|z-w|+C_{3}|z-w|^{m+1}+C_{3}\displaystyle\sum_{k=3}^{m}(\rho^{m-k+1}+\omega)|z-w|^{k}.\end{aligned}

Next, observe that

(6.11)

\displaystyle\begin{aligned} (w-z)&\cdot\big(\bm{H}_{\varphi}(z)-\bm{H}_{\mathfrak{H}}(z_{0})\big)(w-z)\\
&=(w-z)\cdot\big(\bm{H}_{H}(z)-\bm{H}_{\mathfrak{H}}(z_{0})+\kappa\omega\bm{H}_{\psi}(z)\big)(w-z)\\
&\leq C_{1}\rho^{m-1}|z-w|^{2}+\Bigg(\kappa\omega-\displaystyle\frac{\bm{p}\big(\nabla\varphi(z)\big)\cdot\bm{q}_{H}^{(2)}(z)}{\bm{p}\big(\nabla\varphi(z)\big)\cdot\bm{q}_{\psi}^{(2)}(z)}\Bigg)(w-z)\cdot\big(\bm{H}_{\psi}(z)\big)(w-z).\end{aligned}

where for the first statement we applied the definition (
4.7
) of
\varphi
and for the second we applied (
6.4
) and (
6.5
). By the second part of
6.1
, and the fact that
\bm{q}_{\psi}^{(2)}
has all negative entries (by the explicit form (
4.6
) for
\psi
), we have

\displaystyle\Bigg(\kappa\omega-\displaystyle\frac{\bm{p}\big(\nabla\varphi(z)\big)\cdot\bm{q}_{H}^{(2)}(z)}{\bm{p}\big(\nabla\varphi(z)\big)\cdot\bm{q}_{\psi}^{(2)}(z)}\Bigg)\bm{q}_{\psi}^{(2)}(z)\leq\frac{\kappa\omega}{2}\bm{q}_{\psi}^{(2)}(z),\qquad\text{entrywise}.

Since
\bm{q}_{\psi}^{(2)}(t,x)=(-\zeta^{2}e^{\zeta t},0,-\zeta^{2}e^{\zeta x})
, since
\kappa\geq\frac{1}{4}
, and since
|z|\leq 3B_{0}
for any
z\in\mathfrak{P}
(as
|z|\leq\|f\|_{\mathcal{C}^{m+1}}+\|g\|_{\mathcal{C}^{m+1}}+L^{-1}\leq 3B_{0}
), it follows that there is a constant
c_{1}=c_{1}(\varepsilon,m)>0
such that

\displaystyle\Bigg(\kappa\omega-\displaystyle\frac{\bm{p}\big(\nabla\varphi(z)\big)\cdot\bm{q}_{H}^{(2)}(z)}{\bm{p}\big(\nabla\varphi(z)\big)\cdot\bm{q}_{\psi}^{(2)}(z)}\Bigg)(w-z)\cdot\big(\bm{H}_{\psi}(z)\big)(w-z)\leq-c_{1}\omega|z-w|^{2}.

Together with (
6.10
) and (
6.11
), this gives

\displaystyle\varphi(w)-\varphi(z)-\big(\mathfrak{H}(w_{0})-\mathfrak{H}(z_{0})\big)

\displaystyle\leq C_{3}\displaystyle\sum_{k=1}^{m+1}\rho^{m-k+1}|z-w|^{k}+C_{3}m\omega|z-w|^{3}-c_{1}\omega|z-w|^{2}.

This, since
\rho^{m-k+1}|z-w|^{k}+\omega|z-w|^{3}\leq 2n^{-1/m^{2}}\omega|z-w|^{2}
for each
k\in\llbracket 2,m+1\rrbracket
(which holds by (
4.6
), the fact that
|z-w|\leq n^{1/2m^{2}}\rho
, and the fact that
\delta\leq\frac{1}{5m^{2}}
), implies the lemma.
∎

6.2.
Proof of
4.4

In this section we establish
4.4
. As indicated in
Section

6.1
, we only show the bound in that proposition on
\Omega_{j;k}^{+}(z)
, as the proof of the bound on
\Omega_{j;k}^{-}(z)
is entirely analogous; recall we assume that
\Omega^{\complement}
holds. We proceed by comparing the bridges
\bm{x}(t)
with those
\bm{y}(t)
, concentrating around
\mathfrak{H}
, in a local neighborhood (of diameter about
\rho
) around
z
. To implement this, we must reindex the bridges in
\bm{x}
and
\bm{y}
to form
\bm{x}^{\prime}
and
\bm{y}^{\prime}
, defined as follows.

Figure 6
.
For the proof of
4.4
, we compare the non-intersecting Brownian bridges
\bm{x}(t)
in a neighborhood of
z
with bridges
\bm{y}(t)
in one of
z_{0}
.

Let
z=(t,x)\in\mathfrak{P}
and
z_{0}=(\rho,0)\in\mathfrak{S}
; see
Figure

6
for a depiction. Letting
c_{1}=c_{1}(\varepsilon,B,m)>0
denote the constant
c
from
6.3
(and recalling
C_{1}
from (
6.5
)), set

(6.12)

\displaystyle\begin{aligned} &A=\bigg\lfloor n\varphi(z)-\displaystyle\frac{c_{1}\rho^{2}\omega n}{2}\bigg\rfloor;\quad B=\big\lfloor n\mathfrak{H}(z_{0})+n^{\delta}\big\rfloor;\quad\varsigma_{1}=\min\{\rho,t\};\quad\varsigma_{2}=\min\{\rho,L^{-1}-t\};\\
&d_{1}=\min\big\{\lceil 5C_{1}^{3}\rho n\rceil,A-1\big\};\quad d_{2}=\min\big\{\lceil 5C_{1}^{3}\rho n\rceil,n-A\big\};\quad\varsigma=\varsigma_{1}+\varsigma_{2};\quad d=d_{1}+d_{2}.\end{aligned}

In what follows, we omit the floors and ceilings, as they do not affect the proofs; we may also assume that
A\leq n-5n^{\delta}
, for otherwise
\varphi(z)\geq 1+n^{-\delta}\rho^{2}\omega
and so
n^{-1}\mathsf{H}^{x}(z)\leq 1\leq\varphi(z)-n^{-\delta}\rho^{2}\omega=\varphi_{j;k}(z)
holds deterministically. For each integer
j\in\llbracket 0,d+2\rrbracket
and real number
s\in[0,\varsigma]
, set

(6.13)

\displaystyle x_{j}^{\prime}(s)=x_{A+j-d_{1}-1}(s+t-\varsigma_{1})-x;\qquad y_{j}^{\prime}(s)=y_{B+j-d_{1}-1}(s+\rho-\varsigma_{1}),

where
x_{0}=g
and
x_{n+1}=f
. Further denote
\bm{x}^{\prime}=(x_{1}^{\prime},x_{2}^{\prime},\ldots,x_{d+1}^{\prime})\in\llbracket 1,d+1\rrbracket\times\mathcal{C}\big([0,\varsigma]\big)
and
\bm{y}^{\prime}=(y_{1}^{\prime},y_{2}^{\prime},\ldots,y_{d+1}^{\prime})\in\llbracket 1,d+1\rrbracket\times\mathcal{C}\big([0,\varsigma]\big)
. Also define the functions
\mathfrak{x}^{-},\mathfrak{x}^{+},\mathfrak{y}^{-},\mathfrak{y}^{+}:[0,\varsigma]\rightarrow\mathbb{R}
by for each
s\in[0,\varsigma]
setting

\displaystyle\mathfrak{x}^{-}(s)=x_{d+2}^{\prime}(s);\qquad\mathfrak{x}^{+}(s)=x_{0}^{\prime}(s);\qquad\mathfrak{y}^{-}(s)=y_{d+2}^{\prime}(s);\qquad\mathfrak{y}^{+}(s)=y_{0}^{\prime}(s).

Further define the entrance data
\bm{u}^{\prime},\bm{u}^{\prime\prime}\in\overline{\mathbb{W}}_{d+1}
and ending data
\bm{v}^{\prime},\bm{v}^{\prime\prime}\in\overline{\mathbb{W}}_{d+1}
by setting
\bm{u}^{\prime}=\bm{x}^{\prime}(0)
,
\bm{v}^{\prime}=\bm{x}^{\prime}(\varsigma)
,
\bm{u}^{\prime\prime}=\bm{y}^{\prime}(0)
, and
\bm{v}^{\prime\prime}=\bm{y}^{\prime}(\varsigma)
.

Then, the laws of
\bm{x}^{\prime}
and
\bm{y}^{\prime}
are given by non-intersecting Brownian bridges sampled from the measures
\mathfrak{Q}_{\mathfrak{x}^{-};\mathfrak{x}^{+}}^{\bm{u}^{\prime};\bm{v}^{\prime}}(n^{-1})
and
\mathfrak{Q}_{\mathfrak{y}^{-};\mathfrak{y}^{+}}^{\bm{u}^{\prime\prime};\bm{v}^{\prime\prime}}(n^{-1})
, respectively. Denote their height functions (recall (
3.6
)) by
\mathsf{H}^{\bm{x}^{\prime}}:\mathfrak{P}_{\mathfrak{x}^{-};\mathfrak{x}^{+}}\rightarrow\mathbb{R}
and
\mathsf{H}^{\bm{y}^{\prime}}:\mathfrak{P}_{\mathfrak{y}^{-};\mathfrak{y}^{+}}\rightarrow\mathbb{R}
, respectively. Observe by (
6.13
) that for any
(t_{0},x_{0})\in\mathfrak{P}_{\mathfrak{x}^{-},\mathfrak{x}^{+}}\cap\mathfrak{P}_{\mathfrak{y}^{-},\mathfrak{y}^{+}}
we have for any
(t_{0},x_{0})\in[0,\varsigma]\times\mathbb{R}
that

(6.14)

\displaystyle\begin{aligned} &\mathsf{H}^{\bm{x}^{\prime}}(t_{0},x_{0})=\mathsf{H}^{\bm{x}}(t_{0}+t-\varsigma_{1},x_{0}+x)-A+d_{1}+1;\\
&\mathsf{H}^{\bm{y}^{\prime}}(t_{0},x_{0})=\mathsf{H}^{\bm{y}}(t_{0}+\rho-\varsigma_{1},x_{0})-B+d_{1}+1.\end{aligned}

Lemma 6.4
.

There exists a constant
c=c(\varepsilon,B,m)>1
and a coupling between
\bm{x}^{\prime}
and
\bm{y}^{\prime}
such that the following holds. On an event
\mathscr{F}
with
\mathbb{P}[\mathscr{F}]\geq 1-c^{-1}e^{-c(\log n)^{2}}
, we have
x_{j}^{\prime}(s)\leq y_{j}^{\prime}(s)
for each
j\in\llbracket 1,d+1\rrbracket
and
s\in[0,\varsigma]
.

Proof.

By monotonicity (
2.3
), we must show on an event of probability at least
1-c^{-1}e^{-c(\log n)^{2}}
that

(6.15)

\displaystyle\begin{aligned} &x_{j}^{\prime}(0)\leq y_{j}^{\prime}(0),\quad\text{and}\quad x_{j}^{\prime}(\varsigma)\leq y_{j}^{\prime}(\varsigma),\qquad\quad\text{for each $j\in\llbracket 1,d+1\rrbracket$};\\
&x_{0}^{\prime}(s)\leq y_{0}^{\prime}(s),\quad\text{and}\quad x_{d+2}^{\prime}(s)\leq y_{d+2}^{\prime}(s),\quad\text{for each $s\in[0,\varsigma]$}.\end{aligned}

Defining the event

(6.16)

\displaystyle\mathscr{E}=\Bigg\{\displaystyle\sup_{z\in\mathfrak{S}}\big|n^{-1}\mathsf{H}^{\bm{y}}(z)-\mathfrak{H}(z)\big|\leq n^{\delta-1}\Bigg\},\qquad\text{we have}\qquad\mathbb{P}[\mathscr{E}]\geq 1-C_{2}e^{-c_{0}(\log n)^{2}},

since
\mathfrak{H}
is
(C_{2};c_{0};\delta)
-concentrating. On
\mathscr{E}\cap\Omega^{\complement}
, we have

\displaystyle\varphi\big(s,x_{j}(s)\big)\geq\displaystyle\frac{j}{n};\qquad\mathfrak{H}\big(s,y_{j-n^{\delta}}(s)\big)\leq\displaystyle\frac{j}{n},\qquad\text{for any $j\in\llbracket 1,n\rrbracket$},

where the first statement holds from
\Omega^{\complement}
and the second holds from
\mathscr{E}
. Replacing
(j,s)
with
(A+j-d_{1}-1,s+t-\varsigma_{1})
in the former; replacing
(j,s)
with
(B+j+n^{\delta}-d_{1}-1,s)
in latter; and using (
6.13
) and (
6.12
), we find for
j\in\llbracket 1,n\rrbracket
and
s\in[0,\varsigma]
that

\displaystyle\varphi\big(s+t-\varsigma_{1},x_{j}^{\prime}(s)+x\big)\geq\varphi(z)-\displaystyle\frac{c_{1}\rho^{2}\omega}{2}+\displaystyle\frac{j-d_{1}-1}{n};

\displaystyle\mathfrak{H}\big(s+\rho-\varsigma_{1},y_{j}^{\prime}(s)\big)\leq\mathfrak{H}(z_{0})+2n^{\delta-1}+\displaystyle\frac{j-d_{1}-1}{n}.

It follows that

(6.17)

\displaystyle\begin{aligned} \varphi(z)-\varphi\big(s+t-\varsigma_{1},x_{j}^{\prime}(s)+x\big)&\leq\displaystyle\frac{c_{1}\rho^{2}\omega}{2}+\displaystyle\frac{d_{1}-j+1}{n}\\
&\leq\mathfrak{H}(z_{0})-\mathfrak{H}\big(s+\rho-\varsigma_{1},y_{j}^{\prime}(s)\big)+\displaystyle\frac{c_{1}\rho^{2}\omega}{2}+2n^{\delta-1}.\end{aligned}

We next apply
6.3
with that
w_{0}=\big(s+\rho-\varsigma_{1},y_{j}^{\prime}(s)\big)
for either
(j,s)\in\{0,d+2\}\times[0,\varsigma]
or
(j,s)\in\llbracket 1,d+2\rrbracket\times\{0,\varsigma\}
. To that end, we must first verify that
|z_{0}-w_{0}|\leq n^{1/2m^{2}}\rho
. In both cases, we have from the event
\mathscr{E}
from (
6.16
), the first two bounds in (
6.5
), and (
6.12
) that

\displaystyle|z_{0}-w_{0}|\leq\big|y_{j}^{\prime}(s)\big|+\rho

\displaystyle=\big|y_{B+j-d_{1}-1}(s+\rho-\varsigma_{1})\big|+\rho

\displaystyle\leq C_{1}\bigg|\displaystyle\frac{B+j-d_{1}-1}{n}-\mathfrak{H}(s+\rho-\varsigma_{1},0)\bigg|+C_{1}n^{\delta-1}+\rho

\displaystyle\leq C_{1}\bigg|\displaystyle\frac{B+j-d_{1}-1}{n}-\mathfrak{H}(z_{0})\bigg|+C_{1}n^{\delta-1}+2C_{1}^{2}\rho\leq 20C_{1}^{4}\rho\leq n^{1/2m^{2}}\rho,

which establishes the bound
|w_{0}-z_{0}|\leq n^{1/2m^{2}}\rho
. We may thus apply
6.3
with
w_{0}=\big(s+\rho-\varsigma_{1},y_{j}^{\prime}(s)\big)
. By (
6.17
), this yields

(6.18)

\displaystyle\begin{aligned} \varphi(z)-\varphi\big(s+t-\varsigma_{1},x_{j}^{\prime}(s)+x\big)&\leq\mathfrak{H}(z_{0})-\mathfrak{H}(w_{0})+\displaystyle\frac{c_{1}\rho^{2}\omega}{2}+2n^{\delta-1}\\
&\leq\varphi(z)-\varphi\big(s+t-\varsigma_{1},y_{j}^{\prime}(s)+x\big)+\displaystyle\frac{c_{1}\rho^{2}\omega}{2}+2n^{\delta-1}\\
&\qquad-c_{1}\omega|w_{0}-z_{0}|^{2}+c_{1}^{-1}\rho^{m}|w_{0}-z_{0}|.\end{aligned}

If
|w_{0}-z_{0}|\geq\rho
, then since
c_{1}\rho^{2}\omega=c_{1}n^{3\delta-1}\geq 4n^{\delta-1}
and
c_{1}^{-1}\rho^{m}|w_{0}-z_{0}|\leq c_{1}^{-1}n^{1/2m^{2}}\rho^{m+1}\leq c_{1}^{-1}n^{(m+1)\delta-1-1/m+1/2m^{2}}\leq n^{\delta-1}
(by (
4.6
) and the fact that
\delta\leq\frac{1}{5m^{2}}
) it follows that

\displaystyle\varphi\big(s+t-\varsigma_{1},y_{j}^{\prime}(s)+x\big)\leq\varphi\big(s+t-\varsigma_{1},x_{j}^{\prime}(s)+x\big).

Since
\partial_{x}\varphi<0
(by the first part of
6.1
), this yields
y_{j}^{\prime}(s)\geq x_{j}^{\prime}(s)
, confirming (
6.15
).

If instead
|w_{0}-z_{0}|<\rho
, then we can improve (
6.18
) using the fact that
\varphi|_{\partial\mathfrak{P}}=H|_{\partial\mathfrak{P}}
. In particular, assume first in (
6.15
) that
s\in\{0,\varsigma\}
; since the two cases are entirely analogous, we let
s=0
. Since
\varsigma_{1}\leq|w_{0}-z_{0}|<\rho
, it follows from (
6.12
) that
t<\rho
and so
\varsigma_{1}=t
. Then,
\varphi\big(s+t-\varsigma_{1},x_{j}^{\prime}(s)+x\big)=\varphi\big(0,x_{j}^{\prime}(s)+x\big)
. We next claim that

(6.19)

\displaystyle\varphi\big(0,y_{j}^{\prime}(s)+x\big)=H\big(0,y_{j}^{\prime}(s)+x\big)\leq\varphi\big(0^{+},y_{j}^{\prime}(s)+x\big)-c_{2}\omega,\qquad\text{where $c_{2}=\frac{1}{8e^{3B_{0}\zeta}}$}.

Indeed, the first statement follows from the definition of
\varphi
(see below (
4.7
)) with the fact that
\big(0,y_{j}^{\prime}(s)+x\big)\in\partial\mathfrak{P}
. To see the second, first observe that

(6.20)

\displaystyle\sup_{z\in\mathfrak{P}}\psi(z)=\sup_{z\in\mathfrak{P}}(\Upsilon-e^{\zeta t}-e^{\zeta x})\leq 2+\sup_{z\in\mathfrak{P}}(e^{\zeta t}+e^{\zeta x})\leq 2e^{3B_{0}\zeta}+2\leq 4e^{3B_{0}\zeta},

where in the first statement we used the definition (
4.6
) of
\psi
; in the second we used the fact that
\inf_{z\in\mathfrak{P}}\psi(z)=2
; in the third we used the fact that
|t|+|x|\leq\sup_{t\in[0,1/L]}\big|f(t)\big|+\sup_{t\in[0,1/L]}\big|g(t)\big|+L^{-1}\leq 3B_{0}
; and in the fourth we used the fact that
B_{0},\zeta\geq 0
. Next, observe for any
z\in\mathfrak{P}
that

\displaystyle\varphi(z)-H(z)=3-(j+1)\omega+\kappa\omega\psi(z)

\displaystyle\geq\kappa\omega\psi(z)

\displaystyle\geq\displaystyle\frac{\omega}{4}\sup_{z\in\mathfrak{P}}\psi(z)\cdot\frac{\inf_{z\in\mathfrak{P}}\psi(z)}{\sup_{z\in\mathfrak{P}}\psi(z)}\geq\frac{\omega}{4}\cdot\frac{2}{4e^{3B_{0}\zeta}}\geq\frac{\omega}{8e^{3B_{0}\zeta}}=c_{2}\omega,

where the first statement is from (
4.7
); the second is from the fact that
j\leq J=3\omega^{-1}-1
; the third is from the fact
\kappa\geq\frac{1}{4}
; the fourth is from the fact that
\inf_{z\in\mathfrak{P}}\psi(z)\geq 2
(from below equation (
4.6
)) and (
6.20
); and the fifth is from the definition of
c_{2}
as in (
6.19
). This verifies (
6.19
).

The estimate (
6.19
), together with the first bound in (
6.18
), (the
w_{0}=\big(s^{+}-\varsigma_{1}+\rho,y_{j}^{\prime}(s)\big)
case of)
6.3
, and (
4.6
), yields (recalling that
s=0
and
t=\varsigma_{1}
)

\displaystyle\varphi(z)-\varphi\big(

\displaystyle 0,x_{j}^{\prime}(s)+x\big)

\displaystyle\leq\mathfrak{H}(z_{0})-\mathfrak{H}\big(s^{+}+\rho-\varsigma_{1},y_{j}^{\prime}(s)\big)+\displaystyle\frac{c_{1}\rho^{2}\omega}{2}+2n^{\delta-1}

\displaystyle\leq\varphi(z)-\varphi\big(0^{+},y_{j}^{\prime}(s)+x\big)+\displaystyle\frac{c_{1}\rho^{2}\omega}{2}+2n^{\delta-1}-c_{1}\omega|w_{0}-z_{0}|^{2}+c_{1}^{-1}\rho^{m}|w_{0}-z_{0}|

\displaystyle\leq\varphi(z)-\varphi\big(0,y_{j}^{\prime}(s)+x\big)+\displaystyle\frac{c_{1}\rho^{2}\omega-2c_{2}\omega}{2}+2n^{\delta-1}+c_{1}^{-1}\rho^{m+1}\leq\varphi(z)-\varphi\big(0,y_{j}^{\prime}(s)+x\big),

from which it again follows that
y_{j}^{\prime}(s)\geq x_{j}^{\prime}(s)
since
\partial_{x}\varphi<0
.

Now assume that
j\in\{0,d+2\}
; the two cases are again entirely analogous, so let
j=0
. Then,

\displaystyle\rho>y_{0}^{\prime}(s)=y_{B-d_{1}-1}(s+\rho-\varsigma_{1})

\displaystyle\geq\displaystyle\frac{d_{1}}{C_{1}n}-C_{1}\big|n^{-1}B-\mathfrak{H}(s+\rho-\varsigma_{1},0)\big|-C_{1}n^{\delta-1}

\displaystyle\geq\displaystyle\frac{d_{1}}{C_{1}n}-C_{1}\big|\mathfrak{H}(\rho,0)-\mathfrak{H}(s+\rho-\varsigma_{1},0)\big|-2C_{1}n^{\delta-1}

\displaystyle\geq\displaystyle\frac{d_{1}}{C_{1}n}-C_{1}^{2}\rho-2C_{1}n^{\delta-1}.

Here, the first statement follows from the fact that
y_{0}^{\prime}(s)\leq|w_{0}-z_{0}|<\rho
; the second follows from (
6.13
); the third follows from the facts that we are restricting to the event
\mathscr{E}
from (
6.16
), and also from the first two bounds in (
6.5
); the fourth follows from the definition (
6.12
) of
B
; and the fifth follows from the first bound in (
6.5
). Hence,
d_{1}<5C_{1}^{3}\rho n
, implying by (
6.12
) that
d_{1}=A-1
.

We must show that
y_{0}^{\prime}(s)\geq x_{0}^{\prime}(s)
. Since
x_{0}^{\prime}(s)=x_{A-d-1}(s+t-\varsigma_{1})-x=x_{0}(s+t-\varsigma)-x=g(s+t-\varsigma_{1})-x
for each
s\in[0,\varsigma]
, this is equivalent to
y_{B-d_{1}-1}(s+\rho)\geq g(s+t)-x
for each
s\in[-\varsigma_{1},\varsigma_{2}]
. To that end, observe that

(6.21)

\displaystyle\begin{aligned} \mathfrak{H}&(\rho,0)-\varphi(t,x)\\
&\leq\mathfrak{H}\big(s+\rho-\varsigma_{1},g(t+s-\varsigma_{1})-x^{+}\big)-\varphi(t+s-\varsigma_{1},g(t+s-\varsigma_{1})-0^{+}\big)+c_{1}^{-1}n^{1/2m^{2}}\rho^{m+1}\\
&\leq\mathfrak{H}\big(s+\rho-\varsigma_{1},g(s+t-\varsigma_{1})-x\big)-\displaystyle\frac{c_{2}\omega}{2}.\end{aligned}

Here, in the first inequality, we applied
6.3
; in the second, we used the fact that
\varphi\big(t-\varsigma_{1},g(t+s-\varsigma_{1})-0^{+}\big)\geq c_{2}\omega\geq 2c_{1}^{-1}n^{1/2m^{2}}\rho^{m+1}
by (
4.6
) and (
6.19
) (since
\mathfrak{H}\big(t+s-\varsigma_{1},g(t+s-\varsigma_{1})\big)=0
). Moreover,

\displaystyle\mathfrak{H}\big(s+\rho-\varsigma_{1},y_{0}^{\prime}(s)\big)

\displaystyle=\mathfrak{H}\big(s+\rho-\varsigma_{1},y_{B-d_{1}-1}(s+\rho-\varsigma_{1})\big)

\displaystyle\leq n^{-1}(B-d_{1}-1)+n^{\delta-1}=n^{-1}(B-A)+n^{\delta-1},

where in the first statement we used the definition of
y_{j}^{\prime}
from (
6.13
); in the second we used the fact that we are restricting to
\mathscr{E}
(from (
6.16
)); and in the third we used the equality
d_{1}=A-1
. Thus,

\displaystyle\mathfrak{H}\big(s+\rho-\varsigma_{1},y_{0}^{\prime}(s)\big)

\displaystyle\leq n^{-1}(B-A)+n^{\delta-1}

\displaystyle\leq\mathfrak{H}(\rho,0)-\varphi(t,x)+\displaystyle\frac{c_{1}\rho^{2}\omega}{2}+2n^{\delta-1}

\displaystyle\leq\mathfrak{H}\big(s+\rho-\varsigma_{1},g(s+t-\varsigma_{1})-x\big)+\displaystyle\frac{c_{1}\rho^{2}\omega-c_{2}\omega}{2}+2n^{\delta-1}

\displaystyle\leq\mathfrak{H}\big(s-\rho-\varsigma_{1},g(s+t-\varsigma_{1})-x\big).

where in the second bound we used the definitions (
6.12
) of
A
and
B
; in the third we applied (
6.21
); and in the fourth we used the fact that
c_{2}\omega-c_{1}\rho^{2}\omega\geq\frac{c_{2}\omega}{2}>4n^{\delta-1}
for sufficiently large
n
. Since
\partial_{x}\mathfrak{H}<0
, it follows that
y_{0}^{\prime}(s)\geq g(s+t-\varsigma_{1})-x
. In particular,
x_{0}^{\prime}(s)\leq y_{0}^{\prime}(s)
, which again verifies (
6.15
) and thus the lemma.
∎

We can now establish
4.4
.

Proof of
4.4
.

By
6.4
, there exists a constant
c_{2}=c_{2}(\varepsilon,B,m)>0
and an event
\mathscr{F}
with
\mathbb{P}[\mathscr{F}]\geq 1-c_{2}^{-1}e^{-c_{2}(\log n)^{2}}
such that
x_{j}^{\prime}(s)\geq y_{j}^{\prime}(s)
for each
(j,s)\in\llbracket 1,d+1\rrbracket\times[0,\varsigma]
on
\mathscr{F}
. By (
6.16
) (and recalling the event
\mathscr{E}
there), we may assume that
\mathscr{F}\subseteq\mathscr{E}
, for otherwise we can replace
\mathscr{F}
with
\mathscr{E}\cap\mathscr{F}
; we restrict to
\mathscr{F}
in what follows. Observe on
\mathscr{E}
(and thus on
\mathscr{F}
) that
(\varsigma_{1},0)\in\mathfrak{P}_{\mathfrak{y}^{-};\mathfrak{y}^{+}}
, since by (
6.14
) and (
6.12
) we have (as
A\leq n-5n^{\delta}
)

\displaystyle\mathsf{H}^{\bm{y}^{\prime}}(\varsigma_{1},0)=\mathsf{H}^{\bm{y}}(z_{0})-n\big(\mathfrak{H}(z_{0})+n^{\delta-1})+d_{1}+1\in[d_{1}-2n^{\delta}+1,d_{1}+2n^{\delta}+1]\subseteq[1,d+1].

The above coupling thus implies that
\mathsf{H}^{\bm{x}^{\prime}}(\varsigma_{1},0)\leq\mathsf{H}^{\bm{y}^{\prime}}(\varsigma_{1},0)
, so by (
6.14
) we deduce

\displaystyle\mathsf{H}^{\bm{x}}(z)=\mathsf{H}^{\bm{x}^{\prime}}(\varsigma_{1},0)+A-d_{1}-1

\displaystyle\leq\mathsf{H}^{\bm{y}^{\prime}}(\varsigma_{1},0)+A-d_{1}-1

\displaystyle=\mathsf{H}^{\bm{y}}(\rho,0)+A-B\leq n\bigg(\varphi(z)-\displaystyle\frac{c_{1}\rho^{2}\omega}{2}\bigg)+\mathsf{H}^{\bm{y}}(\rho,0)-B.

Since on the event
\mathscr{F}\subseteq\mathscr{E}
, we have
\mathsf{H}^{\bm{y}}(\rho,0)\leq B
, so

\displaystyle n^{-1}\mathsf{H}^{\bm{x}}(z)\leq\varphi(z)-\displaystyle\frac{c_{1}\rho^{2}\omega}{2}\leq\varphi-n^{-\delta}\rho^{2}\omega\psi(z)\leq\varphi_{j;k}^{+}(z),

where in the second bound we used the definition (
4.7
) of
\varphi_{j;k}^{+}
(and the uniform boundedness of
\psi
, from (
4.6
)); this establishes the proposition.
∎

7.
Concentrating Functions With Given Local Profiles

In this section we establish
5.9
.

7.1.
Taylor Approximations Through Dyson Brownian Motion

The following proposition produces measures whose Stieltjes transforms have specified derivatives. In what follows, we recall the Stieltjes transform
m_{\mu}
of any measure
\mu\in\mathscr{P}
from
Section

2.2
.

Proposition 7.1
.

For any integer
m\geq 1
and real numbers
\varpi>0
,
\varepsilon\in(0,1)
, and
A,B>1
, there exists a constant
\delta=\delta(\varepsilon,\varpi,A,B)>0
such that the following holds. Let
\bm{r}=(r_{0},r_{1},r_{2},\ldots,r_{m})\in\mathbb{R}^{m+1}
be an
(m+1)
-tuple of real numbers with
|r_{j}|\leq A
for each
j\in\llbracket 0,m\rrbracket
. Also let
\mu\in\mathscr{P}
be a compactly supported probability measure with density
\varrho:\mathbb{R}\rightarrow\mathbb{R}_{\geq 0}
that is
\mathcal{C}^{m}
in a neighborhood of
0
, satisfying
\varepsilon\leq\varrho(x)\leq B
for each
x\in[-\varpi,\varpi]
;
\varrho(x)=0
for
|x|>\varpi
; and
\big|\Real m_{\mu}(0)\big|\leq A
. There exists a measure
\widetilde{\mu}\in\mathscr{P}_{0}
with density
\widetilde{\varrho}:\mathbb{R}\rightarrow\mathbb{R}_{\geq 0}
compactly supported on
[-\varpi,\varpi]
, satisfying the following two properties.

(1)

We have
\frac{\varepsilon}{2}\leq\widetilde{\varrho}(x)\leq 2B
for each
x\in[-\varpi,\varpi]
, and
\widetilde{\varrho}(x)=\varrho(x)
for each
x\in[-\delta,\delta]
.

(2)

For each integer
k\in\llbracket 0,m\rrbracket
we have

(7.1)

\displaystyle\partial_{z}^{k}m_{\tilde{\mu}}(0)=r_{k}+\mathrm{i}\Imaginary\partial_{z}^{k}m_{\mu}(0).

Proof of
7.1
.

If
\Real m_{\mu}(0)=r_{0}
, then we will establish the lemma with the stronger bound on
\widetilde{\varrho}
given by
\frac{3\varepsilon}{4}\leq\widetilde{\varrho}(x)\leq B+\frac{\varepsilon}{4}
. Let us first reduce to this statement. If
\Real m_{\mu}(0)\neq r_{0}
, then denote
\vartheta=r_{0}-\Real m_{\mu}(0)
; assume that
\vartheta>0
, as the case when
\vartheta<0
is entirely analogous. By our assumption,
\vartheta\leq|r_{0}|+\big|\Real m_{\mu}(0)\big|\leq A+B
. Then replace
\varrho(x)
with
\varrho_{0}:\mathbb{R}\rightarrow\mathbb{R}
, defined by for any
x\in\mathbb{R}
setting

(7.2)

\displaystyle\varrho_{0}(x)=\varrho(x)+\displaystyle\frac{\varepsilon}{4}\cdot\big(\bm{1}(d\leq x\leq e^{8\vartheta/\varepsilon}d)-\bm{1}(e^{-4\vartheta/\varepsilon}d\leq x\leq d)\big),

for any real number
d<e^{-10(A+B)/\varepsilon}\varpi
. Then,
\varrho_{0}
continues to be a probability measure supported in
[-\varpi,\varpi]
and satisfies
\frac{3\varepsilon}{4}\leq\varrho_{0}(x)\leq B+\frac{\varepsilon}{4}
for
x\in[-\varpi,\varpi]
. Moreover,
\varrho_{0}=\varrho
in a neighborhood of
0
, and thus
\Imaginary m(0)=\Imaginary m_{\mu_{0}}(0)
by the first statement of (
2.6
), where
m_{\mu_{0}}
denotes the Stieltjes transform of the measure
\mu_{0}=\varrho_{0}(x)dx\in\mathscr{P}_{0}
. Additionally,

\displaystyle\Real m_{\mu_{0}}(0)=\Real m(0)+\displaystyle\frac{\varepsilon}{4}\cdot(8\varepsilon^{-1}\vartheta-4\varepsilon^{-1}\vartheta)=\Real m(0)+\vartheta=r_{0}.

Thus,
\mu_{0}
and
\varrho_{0}
continue to satisfy the hypotheses of the lemma (after replacing
(\varepsilon,B)
with
\big(\frac{3\varepsilon}{4},B+\frac{\varepsilon}{4}\big)
and altering the
r_{j}
for
j\in\llbracket 1,m\rrbracket
to account for the difference in (
7.2
)) but now with
\Real m_{\mu_{0}}(0)=r_{0}
. It follows that there exists a measure
\widetilde{\mu}
with density
\widetilde{\varrho}
satisfying the statement of the lemma (and in fact the bounds
\frac{\varepsilon}{2}\leq\frac{9\varepsilon}{16}\leq\widetilde{\varrho}(x)\leq B+\frac{7\varepsilon}{16}\leq 2B
).

Thus, we will assume in what follows that
\Real m_{\mu}(0)=r_{0}
. We will take
\widetilde{\varrho}(x)
to be of the form

(7.3)

\displaystyle\widetilde{\varrho}(x)=\varrho(x)+\sum_{\ell=1}^{m+2}c_{\ell}\cdot\bm{1}\big(a(y_{\ell}+y)\leq x\leq a(y_{\ell}+y+b)\big),

where the coefficients
c_{\ell}\in\mathbb{R}
and positive real numbers
a,b,y,y_{\ell}>0
will be chosen later, in a way continuous in the parameters
(\varepsilon,A,B)
, so that their uniform boundedness (fixing
(\varepsilon,A,B)
) follows from compactness. In this way, for any complex number
z\in\mathbb{C}
in a neighborhood of
0
, we have

(7.4)

\displaystyle m_{\tilde{\mu}}(z)=m_{\mu}(z)+\sum_{\ell=1}^{m+2}c_{\ell}\log\left(\frac{a(y_{\ell}+y+b)-z}{a(y_{\ell}+y)-z}\right).

Thus, for any integer
k\geq 1
, we have

(7.5)

\displaystyle\partial_{z}^{k}m_{\tilde{\mu}}(z)=\partial_{z}^{k}m_{\mu}(z)+\sum_{\ell=1}^{m+2}c_{\ell}(-1)^{k-1}(k-1)!\left(\frac{1}{\big(z-a(y_{\ell}+y+b)\big)^{k}}-\frac{1}{\big(z-a(y_{\ell}+y)\big)^{k}}\right).

Since the parameters
a
,
b
,
y
, and
y_{\ell}
are all positive, we have
\widetilde{\varrho}(x)=\varrho(x)
for
x\in\mathbb{R}
in a sufficiently small neighborhood of
0
; this verifies the second part of the first statement of the proposition. Moreover, since by (
2.6
) (as
m_{\mu}
and
m_{\tilde{\mu}}
are well-defined near
0
, by the above) we have
\Imaginary m_{\tilde{\mu}}(x)=\pi\widetilde{\varrho}(x)=\pi\varrho(x)=\Imaginary m_{\mu}(x)
for
x
in a neighborhood of
0
. Differentiating this equation in
x
, it follows that
\partial_{z}^{k}\Imaginary m_{\tilde{\mu}}(0)=\partial_{z}^{k}\Imaginary m_{\mu}(0)
for any integer
k\geq 1
, from which the imaginary part of (
7.1
) follows.

By (
7.5
), the real part of the second part of (
7.1
) holds for any
k\in\llbracket 1,m\rrbracket
if and only if

(7.6)

\displaystyle a^{k}\big(\partial_{z}^{k}\Real m_{\mu}(0)-r_{k}\big)=\sum_{\ell=1}^{m+2}c_{\ell}(k-1)!\left(\frac{1}{(y_{\ell}+y+b)^{k}}-\frac{1}{(y_{\ell}+y)^{k}}\right)

Moreover,
\widetilde{\varrho}
has total mass
1
(that is,
\int_{-\infty}^{\infty}\widetilde{\varrho}(x)dx=1
) and the second part of (
7.1
) holds at
k=0
if and only if

(7.7)

\displaystyle\displaystyle\sum_{\ell=1}^{m+2}c_{\ell}=0,\quad\text{and}\quad\displaystyle\sum_{j=1}^{m+2}c_{\ell}\log\bigg(\displaystyle\frac{y_{\ell}+y+b}{y_{\ell}+y}\bigg)=0,

respectively, by (
7.3
) (since
\varrho
has total mass
1
) and (
7.4
) (since
\Real m_{\mu}=r_{0}
). We denote the
(m+2)
-dimensional vectors
\bm{c}=(c_{1},c_{2},\cdots,c_{m+2})
and
\bm{p}=(p_{1},p_{2},\cdots,p_{m+2})
, where
p_{1}=0=p_{2}
and

\displaystyle p_{k}=\partial_{z}^{k-2}\Real m_{\mu}(0)-r_{k-2},\qquad\text{for each $k\in\llbracket 3,m+2\rrbracket$}.

We also define the
(m+2)\times(m+2)
matrices
\bm{A}=\bm{A}(a)=\diag(a,a,a,a^{2},\ldots,a^{m})
and
\bm{W}(\bm{y},b)=\big\{W_{k,\ell}(\bm{y},b)\big\}_{\ell,k\in\llbracket 1,m+2\rrbracket}
, with entries given by

\displaystyle W_{k,\ell}(\bm{y},b)=(k-3)!\left(\frac{1}{(y_{\ell}+y+b)^{k-2}}-\frac{1}{(y_{\ell}+y)^{k-2}}\right),\quad\text{for $k\in\llbracket 3,m+2\rrbracket$};

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\qquad W_{2,\ell}(\bm{y},b)=1,\qquad W_{1,\ell}(\bm{y},b)=\log\bigg(\displaystyle\frac{y_{\ell}+y+b}{y_{\ell}+y}\bigg),

for each
\ell\in\llbracket 1,m+2\rrbracket
. Then we can rewrite the relations (
7.6
) and (
7.7
) as
\bm{A}\cdot\bm{p}=\bm{W}(\bm{y},b)\cdot\bm{c}
or equivalently as
\bm{c}=\bm{W}(\bm{y},b)^{-1}\bm{A}\cdot\bm{p}
.

Next we show there exists some choice of
y,y_{\ell},b>0
such that
\bm{W}(\bm{y},b)
is invertible. Observe that, for generic
b
, the first row of
\bm{W}(\bm{y},b)
is linearly independent from the bottom
m+1
rows (since the logarithmic function is not rational). It therefore suffices to show that the left-bottom
(m+1)\times(m+1)
block
\bm{W}_{0}(\bm{y},b)
of
\bm{W}(\bm{y},b)
(constituting its
(k,\ell)\in\llbracket 2,m+2\rrbracket\times\llbracket 1,m+1\rrbracket
entries) is invertible. Let us write this block as
\bm{W}_{0}(\bm{y},b)=\bm{V}(\bm{y})-\bm{E}(\bm{y},b)
, where the
(m+1)\times(m+1)
matrices
\bm{V}=\bm{V}(\bm{y})=\big\{V_{k,\ell}(\bm{y})\big\}
and
\bm{E}=\bm{E}(\bm{y},b)=\big\{E_{k,\ell}(\bm{y},b)\big\}
are defined by

\displaystyle V_{1,\ell}(\bm{y})=1,\qquad\text{and}\qquad V_{k,\ell}(\bm{y})=-(k-2)!(y_{\ell}+y)^{1-k},\quad\text{for $k\in\llbracket 2,m\rrbracket$};

\displaystyle E_{1,\ell}(\bm{y},b)=0,\qquad\text{and}\qquad E_{k,\ell}(\bm{y},b)=-(k-2)!(y_{\ell}+y+b)^{1-k},\quad\text{for $k\in\llbracket 2,m\rrbracket$},

for each
\ell\in\llbracket 1,m+1\rrbracket
. Then,
\bm{V}(\bm{y})
is a Vandermonde matrix (up to multiplication by a diagonal matrix), and is therefore invertible if we take
y_{1},y_{2},\ldots,y_{m+1}>0
mutually distinct. Moreover, by taking
b
sufficiently large (and generic) the norm of
\bm{E}(\bm{y},b)
can be taken to be sufficiently small so as to guarantee that
\bm{W}(\bm{y})=\bm{V}(\bm{y})-\bm{E}(\bm{y},b)
remains invertible.

There thus exists a choice of the parameters
y,b,y_{1},y_{2},\ldots,y_{m+1}
such that
\bm{W}_{0}(\bm{y},b)
and thus
\bm{W}(\bm{y},b)
is invertible; then,
\bm{c}=\bm{W}(y)^{-1}\bm{A}\cdot\bm{p}
. By taking
a
sufficiently small, we can ensure that each entry of
\bm{c}
is smaller than
(4m+8)^{-1}\varepsilon
and that
a(y_{\ell}+y+b)\leq\varpi
for each
\ell\in\llbracket 1,m+2\rrbracket
. Since
\varepsilon\leq\varrho(x)\leq B
for
x\in[-\varpi,\varpi]
and
\varrho(x)=0
for
|x|>-\varpi
, it follows from (
7.3
) that
\frac{3\varepsilon}{4}\leq\big|\widetilde{\varrho}(x)\big|\leq B+\frac{\varepsilon}{4}
for
x\in[-\varpi,\varpi]
and that
\widetilde{\varrho}(x)=0
for
|x|>\varpi
. In particular,
\widetilde{\varrho}
is a nonnegative measure satisfying the (previously mentioned improvement of the) first statement of the proposition. That it is a probability measure satisfying (
7.1
) follows from our choices of
y
,
b
, and the
(y_{\ell})
.
∎

The next lemma, which is a quick consequence of
2.17
, indicates that bridge-limiting measures (recall
Definition

3.2
) can be obtained through free convolution (recall
Section

2.2
). In what follows, we recall the rescaled semicircle law
\mu_{\semci}^{(t)}
from (
2.9
).

Lemma 7.2
.

Fix a compactly supported measure
\nu_{0}\in\mathscr{P}_{\fin}
and a real number
t>0
. For any real number
s>0
, denote the measure
\nu_{s}=\nu\boxplus\mu_{\semci}^{(s)}\in\mathscr{P}_{\fin}
. Then,
\bm{\nu}=(\nu_{s})_{s\in[0,t]}
is the bridge-limiting measure process on
[0,t]
with boundary data
(\nu_{0};\nu_{t})
.

Proof.

By
2.12
and
3.5
, we may assume that
\nu_{0}
(and thus
\nu_{s}
for each
s\geq 0
) is a probability measure. Recalling (from
Definition

2.16
) the classical locations
\gamma_{j;n}^{\mu}\in\mathbb{R}
with respect to any probability measure
\mu
, abbreviate
\gamma_{j;n}(s)=\gamma_{j;n}^{(\nu_{s})}
, for each real number
s\geq 0
and integers
n\geq 1
and
j\in\llbracket 1,n\rrbracket
. Further define the
n
-tuple
\bm{u}^{n}=(u_{1}^{n},u_{2}^{n},\ldots,u_{n}^{n})\in\overline{\mathbb{W}}_{n}
by setting
u_{j}^{n}=\gamma_{j;n}(0)
, and let
\bm{u}^{n}(s)
denote the process obtained by running Dyson Brownian motion for time
s
on
\bm{u}
; set
\bm{v}^{n}=\bm{u}^{n}(t)
. Denote
\mu_{s}^{n}=\emp\big(\bm{u}^{n}(s)\big)
for each
s\in[0,t]
(recall (
3.1
)).

By (the
A=2
case of)
2.17
, we have for sufficiently large
n
that, with probability at least
1-n^{-1}
,

(7.8)

\displaystyle\displaystyle\int_{-\infty}^{y-2(\log n)^{5}/n}\nu_{s}(dx)\leq\displaystyle\int_{-\infty}^{y}\mu_{s}^{n}(dx)\leq\displaystyle\int_{-\infty}^{y+2(\log n)^{5}/n}\nu_{s}(dx),\qquad\text{for all $(s,y)\in[0,t]\times\mathbb{R}$}.

Moreover, the second part of
2.15
indicates that, conditional on
\bm{v}^{n}=\bm{u}^{n}(t)
, the law of
\bm{u}^{n}(s)
is given by
\mathfrak{Q}^{\bm{u}^{n};\bm{v}^{n}}
. This, together with (
7.8
),
3.1
, and
Definition

3.2
, indicates that
\bm{\nu}=(\nu_{s})_{s\in[0,t]}
is the bridge-limiting measure process on
[0,t]
with boundary data
(\nu_{0};\nu_{t})
.
∎

7.2.
Proof of
5.9

In this section we establish
5.9
. In what follows, we recall the notation from that proposition; on free convolutions and Stieltjes and Hilbert transforms from
Section

2.2
; on bridge-limiting measure processes from
Definition

3.2
; and on the associated (inverted) height functions from
Definition

3.4
.

The function
\mathfrak{H}
of the proposition will eventually be the height function associated with a bridge-limiting measure process arising from a free convolution (as in
7.2
), to which end we must define the initial data for the free convolution. This will proceed using
7.1
, and so we must first define the real numbers
\bm{r}
and measure
\varrho
appearing there; we begin with the former. Define real numbers
r_{0},r_{1},\ldots,r_{m-1}\in\mathbb{R}
through the equations

(7.9)

\displaystyle\displaystyle\sum_{j=0}^{k-1}\binom{k-1}{j}q_{0}^{(k-j)}r_{j}=q_{1}^{(k)},\qquad\text{for each $k\in\llbracket 1,m\rrbracket$}.

Since
q_{0}^{(1)}\leq-\varepsilon
by (
5.3
), these equations uniquely define the
r_{j}
by induction on
j
. Furthermore, by (
5.3
) and induction on
j
, there exists a constant
C_{2}=C_{2}(\varepsilon,B,m)>1
such that
|r_{j}|<C_{2}
for each
j\in\llbracket 0,m-1\rrbracket
.

Next, observe that there exists a constant
C_{3}=C_{3}(\varepsilon,B,m)>1
and a probability measure
\mu\in\mathscr{P}_{0}
with density
\varrho\in L^{2}(\mathbb{R})
such that
\supp\varrho=[-1,1]
, such that
\|\varrho\|_{\mathcal{C}^{m+3}([-1,1])}\leq C_{2}
and such that

(7.10)

\displaystyle\partial_{x}^{k}\varrho(0)=-q_{0}^{(k+1)},\quad\text{for each $k\in\llbracket 0,m-1\rrbracket$},\qquad\text{and}\qquad C_{3}^{-1}<\varrho(x)\leq C_{3},\quad\text{for each $x\in[-1,1]$}.

Moreover, after increasing
C_{3}
if necessary, it follows from (
2.6
), the fact that
\supp\varrho\subseteq[-1,1]
, and the bound
\|\varrho\|_{\mathcal{C}^{m+3}([-1,1])}\leq C_{2}
, that

\displaystyle\big|\Real m_{\mu}(0)\big|=\pi\big|H\varrho(0)\big|=\bigg|\displaystyle\int_{-1}^{1}\frac{\varrho(x)dx}{x}\bigg|=\bigg|\displaystyle\int_{-1}^{1}\displaystyle\frac{\big(\varrho(x)-\varrho(0)\big)dx}{x}\bigg|\leq 2\|\varrho\|_{\mathcal{C}_{1}([-1,1])}\leq 2C_{2}\leq C_{3}.

This verifies the assumptions in
7.1
. Thus, by
7.1
, there is a constant
c_{1}=c_{1}(\varepsilon,B,m)>1
and a probability measure
\mu_{0}\in\mathscr{P}_{0}
with density
\varrho_{0}\in L^{1}(\mathbb{R})
, such that
\supp\varrho_{0}=[-1,1]
and

(7.11)

\displaystyle\begin{aligned} &(2C_{3})^{-1}\leq\varrho_{0}(x)\leq 2C_{3},\quad\text{for each $x\in[-1,1]$};\qquad\varrho_{0}(x)=\varrho(x),\quad\text{for each $x\in[-c_{1},c_{1}]$};\\
&\partial_{z}^{k}m_{\mu_{0}}(0)=r_{k}+\mathrm{i}\Imaginary\partial_{z}^{k}m_{\mu}(0),\quad\text{for each $k\in\llbracket 0,m\rrbracket$}.\end{aligned}

For each real number
t>0
, define the free convolution measure
\mu_{t}=\mu_{0}\boxplus\mu_{\semci}^{(t)}\in\mathscr{P}_{0}
and the associated density
\varrho_{t}\in L^{1}(\mathbb{R})
satisfying
\mu_{t}(dx)=\varrho_{t}(x)dx
. Define the constant
c_{2}=\frac{c_{1}}{3C_{3}}
, and denote the probabilility measure-valued process
\bm{\mu}=(\mu_{t})_{[0,c_{2}]}
; observe that it is the bridge-limiting measure process on
[0,c_{2}]
with boundary data
(\mu_{0};\mu_{c_{2}})
, by
7.2
. Denote its height function by
H^{\star}:[0,c_{2}]\times\mathbb{R}\rightarrow[0,1]
. We will later define
\mathfrak{H}
as the restriction of
H^{\star}
to a certain strip domain.

Lemma 7.3
.

There exist constants
c_{3}=c_{3}(\varepsilon,B,m)>0
and
C_{4}=C_{4}(\varepsilon,B,m)>1
such that the following statements hold. In what follows, we denote
\mathfrak{U}_{0}=(0,c_{3})\times(-c_{3},c_{3})
.

(1)

For each
z\in\overline{\mathfrak{U}}_{0}
, we have
\partial_{x}H^{\star}\leq-c_{3}
and
\|H^{\star}\|_{\mathcal{C}^{m+2}(\mathfrak{U}_{0})}\leq C_{4}
.

(2)

The function
H^{\star}
satisfies the equation (
3.25
) on
\mathfrak{U}_{0}
.

(3)

The full-
m
local profile of
H^{\star}
satisfies
\lim_{z\rightarrow(0,0)}\bm{Q}_{H^{\star}}^{(m)}(z)=\bm{Q}
.

Proof.

The first statement of the lemma follows from
2.11
, whose conditions are verified by (
7.10
) and the fact that
\|\varrho\|_{\mathcal{C}^{m+1}([-1,1])}\leq C_{3}
. This, together with
3.16
, implies the second statement of the lemma. To verify the third statement observe, since
H^{\star}
satisfies (
3.25
), its local profile at any point is consistent in the sense of
Definition

5.1
. Hence, by
5.4
, to show
\lim_{z\rightarrow(0,0)}\bm{Q}_{H^{\star}}^{(m)}(z)=\bm{Q}
, it suffices to show for each
k\in\llbracket 1,m\rrbracket
that

(7.12)

\displaystyle\displaystyle\lim_{z\rightarrow(0,0)}\partial_{x}^{k}H^{\star}(z)=q_{0}^{(k)},\qquad\text{and}\qquad\displaystyle\lim_{z\rightarrow(0,0)}\partial_{x}^{k-1}\partial_{t}H^{\star}(z)=q_{1}^{(k)}.

The first statement in (
7.12
) holds since
\lim_{z\rightarrow(0,0)}\partial_{x}^{k}H^{\star}(z)=-\partial_{x}^{k-1}\varrho_{0}(0)=-\partial_{x}^{k-1}\varrho(0)=q_{0}^{(k)}
, where the first follows from (
3.3
); the second from (
7.11
); and the third from (
7.10
).

To verify the second statement in (
7.12
), observe that

\displaystyle\displaystyle\lim_{z\rightarrow(0,0)}\partial_{x}^{k-1}\partial_{t}H^{\star}(z)

\displaystyle=\bigg(\partial_{y}^{k-1}\partial_{s}\displaystyle\int_{y}^{\infty}\varrho_{s}(x)dx\bigg)\Bigg|_{s=0,y=0}

\displaystyle=\bigg(\pi\partial_{y}^{k-1}\displaystyle\int_{y}^{\infty}\partial_{x}\big(\varrho_{0}(x)H\varrho_{0}(x)\big)dx\bigg)\Bigg|_{y=0}

\displaystyle=-\pi\bigg(\partial_{y}^{k-1}\big(\varrho_{0}(y)H\varrho_{0}(y)\big)\bigg)\Bigg|_{y=0}=-\pi\displaystyle\sum_{j=0}^{k-1}\binom{k-1}{j}\partial_{x}^{j}H\varrho_{0}(0)\cdot\partial_{x}^{k-j-1}\varrho_{0}(0),

where first statement follows from (
3.3
); the second from (
2.12
); the third from performing the integration (and using the fact that
\supp\varrho_{0}=[-1,1]
); and the fourth from taking the
(k-1)
-fold derivative. Together with (
7.10
), the second statement of (
2.6
), the last statement of (
7.11
), and (
7.9
), this yields

\displaystyle\displaystyle\lim_{z\rightarrow(0,0)}\partial_{x}^{k-1}\partial_{t}H^{\star}(z)=\displaystyle\sum_{j=0}^{k-1}\binom{k-1}{j}q_{0}^{(k-j)}\partial_{x}^{j}\Real m_{\mu_{0}}(0)=\displaystyle\sum_{j=0}^{k-1}\binom{k-1}{j}q_{0}^{(k-j)}r_{j}=q_{1}^{(k)},

confirming the second statement of (
7.12
) and thus the lemma.
∎

Now we can establish
5.9
.

Proof of
5.9
.

Let
G^{\star}
denote the inverted height function associated with
H^{\star}
and, recalling the notation of
7.3
, fix

(7.13)

\displaystyle c_{0}=H^{\star}(0,0);\qquad\nu=\frac{c_{3}^{3}}{4C_{4}^{3}};\qquad c_{4}=\displaystyle\frac{c_{3}^{2}}{2C_{4}}.

For each real number
s\in[0,\nu]
, let
f(s)=G^{\star}(s,c_{0}+c_{4})
and let
g(s)=G^{\star}(s,c_{0}-c_{4})
. Observe that

\displaystyle c_{0}=H^{\star}(0,0)\geq-c_{3}\displaystyle\int_{0}^{c_{3}}\partial_{x}H^{\star}(0,y)dy\geq c_{3}^{2},

by the first part of
7.3
, so
c_{0}\geq c_{3}^{2}\geq 4c_{4}>c_{4}
. Then define the strip domain
\mathfrak{S}=\mathfrak{P}_{f;g}\subset\mathbb{R}^{2}
and the function
\mathfrak{H}:\overline{\mathfrak{S}}\rightarrow\mathbb{R}
by setting
\mathfrak{H}(z)=H^{\star}(z)-c_{0}+c_{4}
for each
z\in\overline{\mathfrak{S}}
. See the left side of
Figure

7
. Let us verify that the conditions of the proposition hold for this choice of
(\mathfrak{S},\mathfrak{H})
.

To see that
[0,\nu]\times[-\nu,\nu]\subseteq\mathfrak{P}_{f;g}\subseteq[0,\nu]\times[-C\nu,C\nu]
, observe again by the first part of
7.3
, and using
H^{\star}(0,0)=c_{0}
and
H^{\star}(0,f(0))=c_{0}+c_{4}
, we have
-\frac{c_{3}}{2C_{4}}=-c_{3}^{-1}c_{4}\leq f(0)\leq-C_{4}^{-1}c_{4}=-\frac{c_{3}^{2}}{2C_{4}^{2}}
. Since
\big|f^{\prime}(s)\big|=\big|(\partial_{t}H^{\star})(s,f(s))\big|\cdot\big|(\partial_{x}H^{\star})(s,f(s))\big|^{-1}\leq c_{3}^{-1}C_{4}
for
s\in[0,\nu]
by the first part of
7.3
, it follows from (
7.13
) that, for each
s\in[0,\nu]
,

\displaystyle-\displaystyle\frac{4C^{2}_{4}\nu}{c^{2}_{3}}\leq-\frac{c_{3}}{2C_{4}}-\frac{C_{4}\nu}{c_{3}}\leq f(s)\leq\frac{C_{4}\nu}{c_{3}}-\frac{c_{3}^{2}}{2C_{4}^{2}}=-\frac{C_{4}\nu}{c_{3}}\leq-\nu.

where we also used
\nu=c_{3}^{3}(4C_{4}^{3})^{-1}
from (
7.13
). Similar reasoning implies that
\nu\leq g(s)\leq 4c_{3}^{-2}C^{2}_{4}\nu
, which indicates that
[0,\nu]\times[-\nu,\nu]\subseteq\mathfrak{P}_{f;g}\subseteq[0,\nu]\times[-C\nu,C\nu]
for
C=4C^{2}_{4}c_{3}^{-2}
.

To establish the other parts of the proposition, observe that the bounds on
-\partial_{x}\mathfrak{H}
and on
\|\mathfrak{H}\|_{\mathcal{C}^{m+1}}
follow from the first part of
7.3
. Moreover, the fact that the full-
m
local profile of
\mathfrak{H}
around
(0,0)
is given by
\bm{Q}
follows from the third part of
7.3
.

It therefore remains to show that
\mathfrak{H}
is
(C_{1};c;\delta)
concentrating in the sense of
Definition

5.8
. That it satisfies (
3.25
) follows from the second part of
7.3
. Now let
n\geq 1
be an integer; set
n^{\prime}=\lfloor 2c_{4}n\rfloor
(in what follows, we again omit the floors and ceilings, as they will not affect the proofs), and define the
n^{\prime}
-tuples
\bm{u},\bm{v}\in\overline{\mathbb{W}}_{n^{\prime}}
by setting

(7.14)

\displaystyle u_{j}=G^{\star}(0,c_{0}-c_{4}+jn^{-1}),\quad\text{and}\quad v_{j}=G^{\star}(\nu,c_{0}-c_{4}+jn^{-1}),\quad\text{for each $j\in\llbracket 1,n^{\prime}\rrbracket$};

and sample
n^{\prime}
non-intersecting Brownian bridges
\bm{y}=(y_{1},y_{2},\ldots,y_{n^{\prime}})\in\llbracket 1,n\rrbracket\times\mathcal{C}\big([0,\nu]\big)
from the measure
\mathfrak{Q}_{f;g}^{\bm{u};\bm{v}}(n^{-1})
. We must show that

(7.15)

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\Bigg[\displaystyle\sup_{z\in\mathfrak{S}}\big|n^{-1}\mathsf{H}^{\bm{y}}(z)-H^{\star}(z)+c_{0}-c_{4}\big|\leq n^{\delta-1}\Bigg]\geq 1-C_{1}e^{-c(\log n)^{2}}.

Figure 7
.
Depicted in the left panel is the strip domain
\mathfrak{S}=\mathfrak{P}_{f;g}
. Depicted in the right panel is the coupling such that
\widetilde{y}_{j}^{-}\leq y_{j}\leq\widetilde{y}_{j}^{+}
.

To that end, define the
n
-tuples
\bm{u}^{-},\bm{u}^{+}\in\overline{\mathbb{W}}_{n}
by for each
j\in\llbracket 1,n\rrbracket
setting

(7.16)

\displaystyle u_{j}^{-}=G^{\star}(0,jn^{-1}+n^{\delta/2-1});\qquad u_{j}^{+}=G^{\star}(0,jn^{-1}-n^{\delta/2-1}).

Let
\bm{y}^{-}(t)=\big(y_{1}^{-}(t),y_{2}^{-}(t),\ldots,y_{n}^{-}(t)\big)
and
\bm{y}^{+}(t)=\big(y_{1}^{+}(t),y_{2}^{+}(t),\ldots,y_{n}^{+}(t)\big)
denote Dyson Brownian motion run with initial data
\bm{u}^{-}
and
\bm{u}^{+}
, respectively. Since
H^{\star}
is the height function associated with the measure-valued process
\big(\mu_{0}\boxplus\mu_{\semci}^{(t)}\big)_{t\in[0,c_{2}]}
,
2.17
gives constants
c=c(\varepsilon,\delta,B,m)>0
and
C_{1}=C_{1}(\varepsilon,\delta,B,m)>1
such that, with probability at least
1-C_{1}e^{-c(\log n)^{2}}
, we have

(7.17)

\displaystyle\begin{aligned} &H^{\star}(t,x)-n^{\delta/2-1}-n^{\delta/4-1}\leq n^{-1}\mathsf{H}^{\bm{y}^{-}}(t,x)\leq H^{\star}(t,x)-n^{\delta/2-1}+n^{\delta/4-1};\\
&H^{\star}(t,x)+n^{\delta/2-1}-n^{\delta/4-1}\leq n^{-1}\mathsf{H}^{\bm{y}^{+}}(t,x)\leq H^{\star}(t,x)+n^{\delta/2-1}+n^{\delta/4-1},\end{aligned}

for any
(t,x)\in\overline{\mathfrak{S}}
. In the below, we restrict to this event.

Since
f(t)=G^{\star}(t,c_{0}-c_{4})
and
g(t)=G^{\star}(t,c_{0}+c_{4})
, applying (
7.17
) with
x
equal to each of
y_{(c_{0}+c_{4})n+1}^{-}(t)
,
y_{(c_{0}+c_{4})n+1}^{+}(t)
,
y_{(c_{0}-c_{4})n}^{-}(t)
,
y_{(c_{0}-c_{4})n}^{+}(t)
,
f(t)
, and
g(t)
(and using the fact that
H^{\star}(t,x)
,
\mathsf{H}^{\bm{y}^{-}}(t,x)
, and
\mathsf{H}^{\bm{y}^{+}}(t,x)
are decreasing in
x
) yields

(7.18)

\displaystyle y_{(c_{0}+c_{4})n+1}^{-}(t)\leq f(t)\leq y_{(c_{0}+c_{4})n+1}^{+}(t);\qquad y_{(c_{0}-c_{4})n}^{-}(t)\leq g(t)\leq y_{(c_{0}-c_{4})n}^{+}(t).

Now, denote
\mathfrak{c}=c_{0}-c_{4}
, and condition on the
y_{j}^{-}(s)
and
y_{j}^{+}(s)
with
j\notin\llbracket\mathfrak{c}n+1,\mathfrak{c}n+n^{\prime}\rrbracket
. Define the functions
\widetilde{f}^{-},\widetilde{f}^{+},\widetilde{g}^{-},\widetilde{g}^{+}:[0,\nu]\rightarrow\mathbb{R}
by setting

\displaystyle\widetilde{f}^{\pm}(s)=y_{\mathfrak{c}n+n^{\prime}+1}^{\pm}(s);\qquad\widetilde{g}^{\pm}(s)=y_{\mathfrak{c}n}^{\pm}(s),

for each index
\pm\in\{+,-\}
and real number
s\in[0,\nu]
. Further define
\widetilde{\bm{y}}^{-}=(\widetilde{y}_{1}^{-},\widetilde{y}_{2}^{-},\ldots,\widetilde{y}_{n^{\prime}}^{-})\in\llbracket 1,n^{\prime}\rrbracket\times\mathcal{C}\big([0,\nu]\big)
and
\widetilde{\bm{y}}^{+}=(\widetilde{y}_{1}^{+},\widetilde{y}_{2}^{+},\ldots,\widetilde{y}_{n^{\prime}}^{+})\in\llbracket 1,n^{\prime}\rrbracket\times\mathcal{C}\big([0,\nu]\big)
by reindexing
\bm{y}^{-}
and
\bm{y}^{+}
, respectively, namely, by setting

(7.19)

\displaystyle\widetilde{y}_{j}^{\pm}(s)=y_{j+\mathfrak{c}n}^{\pm}(s),\qquad\text{for each $\pm\in\{+,-\}$ and $(j,s)\in\llbracket 1,n^{\prime}\rrbracket\times[0,\nu]$},

and define the
n^{\prime}
-tuples
\widetilde{\bm{u}}^{-},\widetilde{\bm{u}}^{+},\widetilde{\bm{v}}^{-},\widetilde{\bm{v}}^{+}\in\overline{\mathbb{W}}_{n^{\prime}}
by setting

(7.20)

\displaystyle\widetilde{u}_{j}^{\pm}=u_{j+\mathfrak{c}n}^{\pm}=\widetilde{y}_{j}^{\pm}(0),\quad\text{and}\quad\widetilde{v}_{j}^{\pm}=\widetilde{y}_{j}^{\pm}(\nu),\qquad\text{for each $\pm\in\{+,-\}$ and $j\in\llbracket 1,n^{\prime}\rrbracket$.}

Then, by the second part of
2.15
, the laws of
\widetilde{\bm{y}}^{-}
and
\widetilde{\bm{y}}^{+}
are given by
\mathfrak{Q}_{\tilde{f}^{-};\tilde{g}^{-}}^{\tilde{\bm{u}}^{-};\tilde{\bm{v}}^{-}}(n^{-1})
and
\mathfrak{Q}_{\tilde{f}^{+};\tilde{g}^{+}}^{\tilde{\bm{u}}^{+};\tilde{\bm{v}}^{+}}(n^{-1})
, respectively; see the right side of
Figure

7
. Observe from (
7.14
), (
7.16
), and (
7.19
) that
\widetilde{\bm{u}}^{-}\leq\bm{u}\leq\widetilde{\bm{u}}^{+}
, since
\partial_{y}G^{\star}\leq 0
. Moreover, (
7.18
) and (
7.19
) imply that
\widetilde{f}^{-}=\widetilde{y}_{n^{\prime}+1}^{-}\leq f\leq\widetilde{y}_{n^{\prime}+1}^{+}=\widetilde{f}^{+}
and
\widetilde{g}^{-}=\widetilde{y}_{0}^{-}\leq g\leq\widetilde{y}_{0}^{+}=\widetilde{g}^{+}
. Additionally, by (
7.14
) and by the
t=\nu
(or
s=\nu
) case of (
7.17
), (
7.19
), (
7.20
), and the first statement of
7.3
, we find that
\bm{\widetilde{v}}^{-}\leq\bm{v}\leq\bm{\widetilde{v}}^{+}
. Together with the monotone coupling
2.3
, these indicate that it is possible to couple
\widetilde{\bm{y}}^{-}
,
\bm{y}
, and
\widetilde{\bm{y}}^{+}
in such a way that
\widetilde{y}_{j}^{-}(s)\leq y_{j}(s)\leq\widetilde{y}_{j}^{+}(s)
for each
(j,s)\in\llbracket 0,n^{\prime}\rrbracket\times[0,\nu]
. This, with (
7.17
) and (
7.19
), yields (
7.15
) and thus the proposition.
∎

8.
Universality of Bulk Statistics

In this section we establish
1.5
.

8.1.
Proof of
1.5

In this section we establish
1.5
. Given
1.4
, its proof will be similar to what was done in
[
2
, Sections 3 and 4]
, by comparing non-intersecting Brownian bridges to Dyson Brownian motion (though the affine invariance of our model will simplify the arguments here). Throughout, we adopt the notation from
1.5
, and we set

(8.1)

\displaystyle\omega=90000(\delta+m^{-1});\qquad n^{\prime}=\lfloor n^{1/2}\rfloor;\qquad n^{\prime\prime}=\lfloor n^{1/4}\rfloor,\qquad s_{0}=n^{\omega/2-1},\qquad s_{1}=n^{3\omega-1},

so that
\omega\leq\frac{1}{20}
(as
m\geq 2^{25}
and
\delta\leq\frac{1}{5m^{2}}
). In what follows, we omit all floors and ceilings, as they do not affect the proofs. We also assume (as we may by shifting) that
G(t_{0}-s_{0},y_{0})=0
.

Define
\breve{\bm{x}}=(\breve{x}_{1},\breve{x}_{2},\ldots,\breve{x}_{n^{\prime}})\in\llbracket 1,n^{\prime}\rrbracket\times\mathcal{C}\big([0,s_{0}+s_{1}]\big)
by reindexing
\bm{x}
, specifically, by setting

\displaystyle\breve{x}_{j}(s)=x_{y_{0}n-n^{\prime}/2+j}(t_{0}-s_{0}+s),\qquad\text{for each $(j,s)\in\llbracket 1,n^{\prime}\rrbracket\times[0,s_{0}+s_{1}]$}.

Also define the
n^{\prime}
-tuples
\breve{\bm{u}}=(\breve{u}_{1},\breve{u}_{2},\ldots,\breve{u}_{n^{\prime}})\in\overline{\mathbb{W}}_{n^{\prime}}
and
\breve{\bm{v}}=(\breve{v}_{1},\breve{v}_{2},\ldots,\breve{v}_{n})\in\overline{\mathbb{W}}_{n^{\prime}}
, and functions
\breve{f},\breve{g}:[0,s_{0}+s_{1}]\rightarrow\mathbb{R}
by for each
j\in\llbracket 1,n^{\prime}\rrbracket
and
s\in[0,s_{0}+s_{1}]
setting

\displaystyle\breve{u}_{j}=\breve{x}_{j}(0);\qquad\breve{v}_{j}=\breve{x}_{j}(s_{0}+s_{1});\qquad\breve{f}(s)=\breve{x}_{n^{\prime}/2+n^{\prime\prime}+1}(s);\qquad\breve{g}(s)=\breve{x}_{n^{\prime}/2-n^{\prime\prime}-1}(s).

Next, we condition on
\breve{x}_{j}(s)
for
(j,s)\notin\big\llbracket\frac{n^{\prime}}{2}-n^{\prime\prime},\frac{n^{\prime}}{2}+n^{\prime\prime}\rrbracket\times(0,s_{0}+s_{1})
. Defining the event

(8.2)

\displaystyle\mathscr{A}_{0}=\bigcap_{(j,s)\notin\llbracket n^{\prime}/2-n^{\prime\prime},n^{\prime}/2+n^{\prime\prime}\rrbracket\times(0,s_{0}+s_{1})}\Bigg\{\bigg|\breve{x}_{j}(s)-G\Big(t_{0}-s_{0}+s,y_{0}-\displaystyle\frac{n^{\prime}}{2n}+\displaystyle\frac{j}{n}\Big)\bigg|\leq n^{\omega/4-1}\Bigg\},

we have by
1.4
and the fact that
n^{\omega/4-1}\geq\varkappa+n^{2/m+3\delta/2-1}
(by (
8.1
) and the fact that
\varkappa\leq n^{\delta-1}
) that there exists a constant
c_{0}=c_{0}(\varepsilon,\delta,B_{0},m)>0
such that

(8.3)

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}[\mathscr{A}_{0}]\geq 1-c_{0}^{-1}e^{-c_{0}(\log n)^{2}}.

We restrict to
\mathscr{A}_{0}
in what follows.

Next, for each index
\pm\in\{+,-\}
, let
G^{\pm}:[0,1]\rightarrow\mathbb{R}
be a function satisfying the following six properties.

(1)

For all
y\in[0,1]
, we have
G(t_{0}-s_{0},y)-n^{\omega/2-1}\leq G^{-}(y)\leq G(t_{0}-s_{0},y)
and that
G(t_{0}-s_{0},y)\leq G^{+}(y)\leq G(t_{0}-s_{0},y)+n^{\omega/2-1}
.

(2)

For all
y\in[0,1]
with
|y-y_{0}|\leq\frac{5n^{\prime\prime}}{6n}
, we have
G(s_{0}-t_{0},y)-n^{3\omega/10-1}\leq G^{-}(y)\leq G(t_{0}-s_{0},y)
and that
G(t_{0}-s_{0},y)\leq G^{+}(y)\leq G(t_{0}-s_{0},y)+n^{3\delta/10-1}
.

(3)

For
y\in[0,1]
with
|y-y_{0}|\leq\frac{n^{\prime\prime}}{2n}
, we have
G^{\pm}(y)=G(t_{0}-s_{0},y)
.

(4)

For all
y\in[0,1]
with
|y-y_{0}|\in\big[\frac{2n^{\prime\prime}}{3n},\frac{5n^{\prime\prime}}{6n}\big]
, we have
G^{\pm}(t_{0}-s_{0},y)=G(t_{0}-s_{0},y)\pm n^{3\omega/10-1}
.

(5)

For all
y\in[0,1]
with
|y-y_{0}|\geq\frac{n^{\prime\prime}}{n}
, we have
G^{\pm}(y)=G(t_{0}-s_{0},y)\pm n^{\omega/2-1}
.

(6)

For all
y\in[0,1]
, we have
-\frac{2}{\varepsilon}\leq\partial_{y}G^{\pm}(y)\leq-\frac{\varepsilon}{2}
, and
\|G^{\pm}\|_{\mathcal{C}^{m+1}}\leq 2B_{0}
.

The existence of such a function follows from the facts that
n^{\omega-1}\leq n^{-3/4}=\frac{n^{\prime\prime}}{n}
, that
G\in\Adm_{\varepsilon}(\mathfrak{R})
, and that
\|G\|_{\mathcal{C}^{m+1}(\mathfrak{R})}\leq B_{0}
(by
1.3
). Then, define the
n^{\prime}
-tuples
\breve{\bm{u}}^{-},\breve{\bm{u}}^{+}\in\overline{\mathbb{W}}_{n^{\prime}}
by, for each index
\pm\in\{+,-\}
and integer
j\in\llbracket 1,n^{\prime}\rrbracket
, setting

(8.4)

\displaystyle\breve{u}_{j}^{\pm}=\breve{u}_{j},\quad\text{if $j\in\Big\llbracket\displaystyle\frac{n^{\prime}}{2}-\displaystyle\frac{2n^{\prime\prime}}{3},\displaystyle\frac{n^{\prime}}{2}+\displaystyle\frac{2n^{\prime\prime}}{3}\Big\rrbracket$};\qquad\breve{u}_{j}^{\pm}=G^{\pm}\Big(y_{0}-\displaystyle\frac{n^{\prime}}{2n}+\displaystyle\frac{j}{n}\Big),\quad\text{otherwise},

observing that
\bm{u}^{\pm}\in\overline{\mathbb{W}}_{n^{\prime}}
on
\mathscr{A}_{0}
due to (
8.2
).

Let
\breve{\bm{w}}(s)=\big(\breve{w}_{1}(s),\breve{w}_{2}(s),\ldots,\breve{w}_{n^{\prime}}(s)\big)
denote Dyson Brownian motion with initial data
\breve{\bm{u}}
, as in (
2.16
), but with the
d\breve{w}_{i}
there multiplied by a factor of
n^{-1}n^{\prime}
and the
dB_{i}
multiplied by a factor of
(n^{-1}n)^{1/2}
; more specifically, they solve (instead of (
2.16
)) the stochastic differential equation

\displaystyle d\breve{w}_{i}(s)=n^{-1}\displaystyle\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}1\leq j\leq n^{\prime}\\
j\neq i\end{subarray}}\displaystyle\frac{1}{\breve{w}_{i}^{\pm}(s)-\breve{w}_{j}^{\pm}(s)}+n^{-1/2}dB_{i}(s).

Similarly, for each
\pm\in\{+,-\}
, let
\breve{\bm{w}}^{\pm}(s)=\big(\breve{w}_{1}^{\pm}(s),\breve{w}_{2}^{\pm}(s),\ldots,\breve{w}_{n^{\prime}}^{\pm}(s)\big)
denote Dyson Brownian motion with initial data
\breve{\bm{u}}^{\pm}
, but with the same rescaling of the
d\breve{w}_{i}^{\pm}(s)
as above; see
Figure

8
. Moreover define the functions
\breve{f}^{-},\breve{f}^{+},\breve{g}^{-},\breve{g}^{+}:[0,s_{0}+s_{1}]\rightarrow\mathbb{R}
by, for each index
\pm\in\{+,-\}
and real number
s\in[0,s_{0}+s_{1}]
, setting

\displaystyle\breve{f}^{\pm}(s)=\breve{w}_{n^{\prime}/2+n^{\prime\prime}+1}^{\pm}(s);\qquad\breve{g}^{\pm}(s)=\breve{w}_{n^{\prime}/2-n^{\prime\prime}-1}^{\pm}(s).

We then have the following proposition, to be established in
Section

8.2
below; it indicates that the middle paths in
\breve{\bm{x}}
are bounded between those in
\breve{\bm{w}}^{-}
and
\breve{\bm{w}}^{+}
(after adding a suitable drift).

Figure 8
.
Shown above are the paths of
\breve{\bm{w}}^{+}
and
\breve{\bm{w}}
in the left panel, and of
\breve{\bm{w}}^{-}
and
\breve{\bm{w}}
in the right panel.

Proposition 8.1
.

Set
\mathfrak{a}=\partial_{t}G(t_{0},y_{0})
. There exist constants
c=c(\varepsilon,\omega,m,B_{0})>0
and
C=C(\varepsilon,\omega,m,B_{0})>1
; a coupling between
\breve{\bm{x}}
,
\breve{\bm{w}}^{-}
, and
\breve{\bm{w}}^{+}
; and an event
\mathscr{A}_{1}
, with
\mathbb{P}[\mathscr{A}_{1}]\geq 1-Ce^{-c(\log n)^{2}}
, such that on
\mathscr{A}_{1}
we have

\displaystyle\breve{w}_{j}^{-}(s)+(\mathfrak{a}-n^{-\omega})s\leq\breve{x}_{j}(s)\leq\breve{w}_{j}^{+}(s)+(\mathfrak{a}+n^{-\omega})s,

for each
(j,s)\in\big\llbracket\frac{n^{\prime}}{2}-n^{\prime\prime},\frac{n^{\prime}}{2}+n^{\prime\prime}\big\rrbracket\times[0,s_{0}+s_{1}]
.

We next have the following three lemmas, whose proofs are both consequences of results in
[
50
,
49
]
and are therefore deferred to
Appendix

D
below. The first, to be established in
Section

D.1
below, indicates that the local statistics of
\breve{\bm{w}}
are prescribed by the sine process. The second, to be established in
Section

D.2
below, indicates that the middle paths of
\breve{\bm{w}}^{-}
and
\breve{\bm{w}}^{+}
are close to those of
\breve{\bm{w}}
. The third, to be established in
Section

D.3
below, states that
\breve{\bm{w}}^{-}
and
\breve{\bm{w}}^{+}
are likely close in expectation, under any coupling between them.

Lemma 8.2
.

Let
\mathfrak{a}>0
be a (bounded, uniformly in
n
) constant, and denote the
n
-tuple
\bm{w}=\theta n\cdot\big(\breve{\bm{w}}(s_{0})+\mathfrak{a}s_{0}\big)\in\overline{\mathbb{W}}_{n}
. We have

\displaystyle\displaystyle\lim_{n\rightarrow\infty}\displaystyle\int_{\mathbb{R}^{k}}F(\bm{a})p_{\bm{w}}^{(k)}(\bm{a})d\bm{a}=\displaystyle\int_{\mathbb{R}^{k}}F(\bm{a})p_{\sin}^{(k)}(\bm{a})d\bm{a}.

Lemma 8.3
.

There exist constants
c=c(\varepsilon,\delta,B_{0},m)>0
and
C=C(\varepsilon,\delta,B_{0},m)>1
; a coupling between
\breve{\bm{w}}^{-}
,
\breve{\bm{w}}
; and
\breve{\bm{w}}^{+}
; and an event
\mathscr{A}_{2}
, with
\mathbb{P}[\mathscr{A}_{2}]\geq 1-Cn^{-c}
, such that on
\mathscr{A}_{2}
we have

(8.5)

\displaystyle\begin{aligned} &\displaystyle\max_{j\in\llbracket n^{\prime}/2-n^{\omega/15000},n^{\prime}/2+n^{\omega/15000}\rrbracket}\big|\breve{w}_{j}(s_{0})-\breve{w}_{j}^{-}(s_{0})\big|\leq n^{-1-\omega/5};\\
&\displaystyle\max_{j\in\llbracket n^{\prime}/2-n^{\omega/15000},n^{\prime}/2+n^{\omega/15000}\rrbracket}\big|\breve{w}_{j}(s_{0})-\breve{w}_{j}^{+}(s_{0})\big|\leq n^{-1-\omega/5}.\end{aligned}

Lemma 8.4
.

For any real number
\chi>0
, there exist constants
c=c(\varepsilon,\delta,B_{0},m)>0
and
C=C(\chi,\varepsilon,\delta,B_{0},m)>1
such that, under any coupling between
\breve{\bm{w}}^{-}
and
\breve{\bm{w}}^{+}
, we have

\displaystyle\displaystyle\max_{j\in\llbracket n^{\prime}/2-n^{\omega/15000},n^{\prime}/2+n^{\omega/15000}\rrbracket}\mathbb{E}\Big[\big|\breve{w}_{j}^{+}(s_{0})-\breve{w}_{j}^{-}(s_{0})\big|\cdot\textbf{\emph{1}}_{|\breve{w}_{j}^{+}(s_{0})-\breve{w}_{j}^{-}(s_{0})|>n^{\chi-1}}\Big]<Ce^{-c(\log n)^{2}}.

Given the above four results, we can establish
1.5
.

Proof of
1.5
.

By a Taylor expansion (and the fact that
G(t_{0}-s_{0},y_{0})=0
), we have

(8.6)

\displaystyle\big|G(t_{0},y_{0})-\mathfrak{a}s_{0}\big|=\big|G(t_{0},y_{0})-G(t_{0}-s_{0},y_{0})-\mathfrak{a}s_{0}\big|\leq B_{0}s_{0}^{2}\leq n^{-1-\omega}.

So, it suffices by the continuity of
F
to show that

(8.7)

\displaystyle\displaystyle\lim_{n\rightarrow\infty}\displaystyle\int_{\mathbb{R}^{k}}F(\bm{a})p_{\theta n\cdot(\breve{\bm{x}}(s_{0})-\mathfrak{a}s_{0})}^{(k)}(\bm{a})d\bm{a}=\displaystyle\int_{\mathbb{R}^{k}}F(\bm{a})p_{\sin}^{(k)}(\bm{a})d\bm{a}.

We next claim that there exist constants
c=c(\varepsilon,\delta,B_{0},m)\in\big(0,\frac{\omega}{30000}\big)
and
C=C(\varepsilon,\delta,B_{0},m)>1
, and a coupling between
\bm{x}
,
\breve{\bm{w}}^{-}
, and
\breve{\bm{w}}^{+}
, such that

(8.8)

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}[\mathscr{A}]\geq 1-Cn^{-c},\qquad\text{where}\quad\mathscr{A}=\mathscr{A}_{0}^{\prime}\cap\mathscr{A}_{1}\cap\mathscr{A}_{3},

where we have recalled
\mathscr{A}_{1}
from
8.1
and defined the events

\displaystyle\mathscr{A}_{0}^{\prime}=\bigcap_{|j-y_{0}n|\leq n^{\omega/15000}}\Big\{\big|x_{j}(s_{0})-G(s_{0},jn^{-1})\big|\leq n^{\omega/30000-1}\Big\};

\displaystyle\mathscr{A}_{3}=\bigcap_{|j-y_{0}n|\leq n^{\omega/15000}}\Big\{\big|\breve{w}_{j}^{-}(s_{0})-\breve{w}_{j}^{+}(s_{0})\big|\leq n^{-1-c}\Big\}.

Let us establish the theorem assuming (
8.8
); throughout this proof, we set
\omega^{\prime}=\frac{\omega}{30000}
. Since
\lim_{n\rightarrow\infty}\mathbb{P}[\mathscr{A}]=1
, we may restrict to the event
\mathscr{A}
in what follows. Setting
\mathfrak{a}=\partial_{t}G(t_{0},y_{0})
and applying the definition of
\mathscr{A}_{1}
from
8.1
(at
s=s_{0}=n^{\omega/2-1}
) gives

(8.9)

\displaystyle\breve{w}_{j}^{-}(s_{0})+\mathfrak{a}s_{0}-n^{-1-\omega/2}\leq\breve{x}_{j}(s_{0})\leq\breve{w}_{j}^{+}(s_{0})+\mathfrak{a}s_{0}+n^{-1-\omega/2},

for each integer
j\in\big\llbracket\frac{n^{\prime}}{2}-n^{\prime\prime},\frac{n^{\prime}}{2}+n^{\prime\prime}\big\rrbracket
. Further observe on
\mathscr{A}_{0}^{\prime}
that for
j>\frac{n^{\prime}}{2}+n^{2\omega^{\prime}}
we have
\breve{x}_{j}(s_{0})-\mathfrak{a}s_{0}\leq G(t_{0},y_{0}+n^{2\omega^{\prime}-1})-\mathfrak{a}s_{0}+n^{\omega^{\prime}-1}\leq n^{-1-\omega}-\varepsilon n^{2\omega^{\prime}-1}+n^{\omega^{\prime}-1}<-n^{\omega^{\prime}-1}
(where we have used (
8.6
) and the fact from
1.3
that
\partial_{y}G\leq-\varepsilon
). By similar reasoning, we have on
\mathscr{A}_{0}
that for
j<\frac{n^{\prime}}{2}-n^{2\omega^{\prime}}
that
\breve{x}_{j}(s_{0})>n^{\omega^{\prime}-1}
. By these two bounds, together with (
8.9
) and the fact that
\big|\breve{w}_{j}^{-}(s_{0})-\breve{w}_{j}^{+}(s_{0})\big|\leq n^{-1-c}
for
j\in\llbracket\frac{n^{\prime}}{2}-n^{2\omega^{\prime}},\frac{n^{\prime}}{2}+n^{2\omega^{\prime}}\big\rrbracket
on
\mathscr{A}
, it follows from the continuity of
F
that, to verify (
8.7
), it suffices to show

\displaystyle\displaystyle\lim_{n\rightarrow\infty}\displaystyle\int_{\mathbb{R}^{k}}F(\bm{a})p_{\theta n\cdot\breve{\bm{w}}^{-}(s_{0})}^{(k)}(\bm{a})d\bm{a}=\displaystyle\int_{\mathbb{R}^{k}}F(\bm{a})p_{\sin}^{(k)}(\bm{a})d\bm{a}.

This follows from the first statement of (
8.5
) and
8.2
(with the continuity of
F
).

It remains to confirm (
8.8
). To that end, it suffices by a union bound to show that
\mathbb{P}[\mathscr{A}_{0}^{\prime}]\geq 1-Cn^{-c}
, that
\mathbb{P}[\mathscr{A}_{1}]\geq 1-Cn^{-c}
, and that
\mathbb{P}\big[\mathscr{A}_{1}\cap\mathscr{A}_{3}^{\complement}\big]\leq Cn^{-c}
. The first follows by
1.4
, since
\omega^{\prime}=3(m^{-1}+\delta)>2m^{-1}+3\delta
, and the second follows from
8.1
.

The third will follow from (
8.5
),
8.4
, and a Markov estimate. To implement this, observe that (
8.5
) and
8.4
together imply the existence of constants
c_{1}=c_{1}(\varepsilon,\delta,B_{0},m)>0
and
C_{1}=C_{1}(\varepsilon,\delta,B_{0},m)>1
, and a coupling between
\breve{\bm{w}}^{-}
and
\breve{\bm{w}}^{+}
(possibly different from the one above), such that

\displaystyle\displaystyle\sum_{|j-n^{\prime}/2|\leq n^{\omega/15000}}\mathbb{E}\Big[\big|\breve{w}_{j}^{+}(s_{0})-\breve{w}_{j}^{-}(s_{0})\big|\Big]\leq C_{1}n^{-1-c_{1}},

from which it follows that

(8.10)

\displaystyle\displaystyle\sum_{|j-n^{\prime}/2|\leq n^{\omega/15000}}\Big(\mathbb{E}\big[\breve{w}_{j}^{+}(s_{0})\big]-\mathbb{E}\big[\breve{w}_{j}^{-}(s_{0})\big]\Big)\leq C_{1}n^{-1-c_{1}}.

Since this bound is independent of the coupling between
\breve{\bm{w}}^{+}
and
\breve{\bm{w}}^{-}
, it also holds for our original one given by
8.1
. Next, restricting to the event
\mathscr{A}_{1}
, we have
\breve{w}_{j}^{-}(s_{0})\leq\breve{w}_{j}^{+}(s_{0})+n^{-\omega}s_{0}=\breve{w}_{j}^{+}(s_{0})+n^{-1-\omega/2}
. Together with (
8.10
), this implies under our original coupling that

\displaystyle\displaystyle\sum_{|j-n^{\prime}/2|\leq n^{\omega/15000}}

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\Big[\textbf{1}_{\mathscr{A}_{1}}\cdot\big|\breve{w}_{j}^{+}(s_{0})-\breve{w}_{j}^{-}(s_{0})\big|\Big]

\displaystyle\leq\displaystyle\sum_{|j-n^{\prime}/2|\leq n^{\omega/15000}}\Big(\mathbb{E}\big[\breve{w}_{j}^{+}(s_{0})\big]-\mathbb{E}\big[\breve{w}_{j}^{-}(s_{0})\big]+2n^{-1-\omega/2}\Big)\leq 3C_{1}n^{-1-c_{2}},

for some constant
c_{2}=c_{2}(\varepsilon,\delta,B_{0},m)>0
. By a Markov estimate, we thus have for
c=\frac{c_{2}}{2}
that

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\big[\mathscr{A}_{1}\cap\mathscr{A}_{3}^{\complement}\big]\leq n^{1+c_{2}/2}\cdot\displaystyle\sum_{|j-n^{\prime}/2|\leq n^{\omega/15000}}\mathbb{E}\Big[\textbf{1}_{\mathscr{A}_{1}}\cdot\big|\breve{w}_{j}^{+}(s_{0})-\breve{w}_{j}^{-}(s_{0})\big|\Big]

\displaystyle\leq 3C_{1}n^{-1-c_{2}/2}.

This gives the third bound above,
\mathbb{P}\big[\mathscr{A}_{1}\cap\mathscr{A}_{3}^{\complement}\big]\leq Cn^{-c}
, and thus the theorem.
∎

8.2.
Proof of
8.1

In this section we establish
8.1
; throughout, we recall the notation of that proposition. We first rescale
s_{0}
,
s_{1}
,
\breve{\bm{x}}(s)
,
\breve{\bm{w}}^{-}(s)
,
\breve{\bm{w}}(s)
,
\breve{\bm{w}}^{+}(s)
,
G
,
G^{-}
, and
G^{+}
to form
\widetilde{s}_{0}
,
\widetilde{s}_{1}
,
\widetilde{\bm{x}}(s)
,
\widetilde{\bm{w}}^{-}(s)
,
\widetilde{\bm{w}}(s)
,
\widetilde{\bm{w}}^{+}(s)
,
\widetilde{G}
,
\widetilde{G}^{-}
, and
\widetilde{G}^{+}
, respectively, defined by setting

(8.11)

\displaystyle\begin{aligned} \widetilde{s}_{i}=\displaystyle\frac{ns_{i}}{n^{\prime}};\qquad\widetilde{z}_{j}(s)=\displaystyle\frac{n}{n^{\prime}}\cdot\breve{z}_{j}\Big(&\displaystyle\frac{n^{\prime}s}{n}\Big);\qquad\widetilde{G}(s,y)=\displaystyle\frac{n}{n^{\prime}}\cdot G\Big(\displaystyle\frac{n^{\prime}s}{n}+t_{0}-s_{0},\displaystyle\frac{n^{\prime}y}{n}+y_{0}-\displaystyle\frac{n^{\prime}}{2n}\Big),\\
\widetilde{G}^{\pm}(&y)=\displaystyle\frac{n}{n^{\prime}}\cdot G^{\pm}\Big(\displaystyle\frac{n^{\prime}y}{n}+y_{0}-\displaystyle\frac{n^{\prime}}{2n}\Big),\end{aligned}

for any integers
i\in\{0,1\}
and
j\in\llbracket 1,n^{\prime}\rrbracket
; indices
z\in\{x,w^{-},w,w^{+}\}
and
\pm\in\{+,-\}
; and real numbers
s\in[0,\widetilde{s}_{0}+\widetilde{s}_{1}]
and
y\in\big[1-y_{0}n^{1/2},(1-y_{0})n^{1/2}\big]
. In this way,
\widetilde{\bm{w}}(s)
is obtained by applying Dyson Brownian motion (
2.16
) to the initial data
\widetilde{\bm{w}}(0)
for time
s
(without the additional rescaling described in
Section

8.1
); similar statements are true for
\bm{w}^{-}(s)
and
\bm{w}^{+}(s)
. By (
8.2
), and the fact that
(n^{\prime})^{2}=n
, we have

(8.12)

\displaystyle\mathscr{A}_{0}=\bigcap_{(j,s)\notin\llbracket n^{\prime}/2-n^{\prime\prime},n^{\prime}/2+n^{\prime\prime}\rrbracket\times(0,\tilde{s}_{0}+\tilde{s}_{1})}\Bigg\{\bigg|\widetilde{x}_{j}(s)-\widetilde{G}\Big(s,\displaystyle\frac{j}{n^{\prime}}\Big)\bigg|\leq(n^{\prime})^{\omega/2-1}\Bigg\}.

Further observe since
G\in\Adm_{\varepsilon/2}(\mathfrak{R})
; since
-\frac{2}{\varepsilon}\leq\partial_{y}G^{\pm}\leq\frac{\varepsilon}{2}
; since
G(t_{0}-s_{0},y_{0})=0
; since
\|G\|_{\mathcal{C}^{m+1}(\mathfrak{R})}\leq B_{0}
; and since
\|G^{\pm}\|_{\mathcal{C}^{m+1}}\leq 2B_{0}
that, for each
\pm\in\{+,-\}
and
k\in\llbracket 1,m+1\rrbracket
,

(8.13)

\displaystyle\begin{aligned} &\widetilde{G}\in\Adm_{\varepsilon/2}\Big(\displaystyle\frac{n}{n^{\prime}}\cdot\mathfrak{R}\Big);\qquad\qquad-\displaystyle\frac{2}{\varepsilon}\leq\partial_{y}\widetilde{G}^{\pm}(y)\leq-\displaystyle\frac{\varepsilon}{2},\quad\text{for $y\in\big[1-y_{0}n^{1/2},(1-y_{0})n^{1/2}\big]$};\\
&\widetilde{G}\Big(0,\displaystyle\frac{1}{2}\Big)=\widetilde{G}^{\pm}\Big(0,\displaystyle\frac{1}{2}\Big)=0;\qquad[\widetilde{G}]_{k},[\widetilde{G}^{\pm}]_{k}\leq 2B_{0}\Big(\displaystyle\frac{n^{\prime}}{n}\Big)^{k-1},\end{aligned}

The proof of
8.1
will make use of the following lemma, to be established in
Section

8.3
below, that approximates the locations of the middle paths in
\widetilde{\bm{w}}^{-}(s)
and
\widetilde{\bm{w}}^{+}(s)
.

Lemma 8.5
.

There exists a coupling between
\widetilde{\bm{w}}^{-}
,
\widetilde{\bm{w}}
, and
\widetilde{\bm{w}}^{+}
, and a constant
c=c(\varepsilon,\delta,B_{0},m)>0
such that
\mathbb{P}[\mathscr{A}_{1}]\geq 1-c^{-1}e^{-c(\log n)^{2}}
, where we have defined the event

(8.14)

\displaystyle\begin{aligned} \mathscr{A}_{1}&=\Bigg\{\displaystyle\sup_{s\in[0,\tilde{s}_{0}+\tilde{s}_{1}]}\bigg(\displaystyle\max_{j\in\llbracket n^{\prime}/2-2n^{\prime\prime},n^{\prime}/2+2n^{\prime\prime}\rrbracket}\big|\widetilde{w}_{j}^{-}(s)-\widetilde{w}_{j}^{-}(0)\big|\bigg)\leq(n^{\prime})^{2\omega/3-1}\Bigg\}\\
&\qquad\cap\Bigg\{\displaystyle\sup_{s\in[0,\tilde{s}_{0}+\tilde{s}_{1}]}\bigg(\displaystyle\max_{j\in\llbracket n^{\prime}/2-2n^{\prime\prime},n^{\prime}/2+2n^{\prime\prime}\rrbracket}\big|\widetilde{w}_{j}^{+}(s)-\widetilde{w}_{j}^{+}(0)\big|\bigg)\leq(n^{\prime})^{2\omega/3-1}\Bigg\}\cap\mathscr{A}_{0}.\end{aligned}

Using
8.5
, we can establish
8.1
.

Proof of
8.1
.

Fix
\mathfrak{a}=\partial_{t}G(t_{0},y_{0})
, which is uniformly bounded in
[-B_{0},B_{0}]
by
1.3
. We claim on the event
\mathscr{A}_{1}
from (
8.14
) that

(8.15)

\displaystyle\begin{aligned} &\breve{w}_{j}^{-}(0)\leq\breve{u}_{j}\leq\breve{w}_{j}^{+}(0);\\
&\breve{w}_{j}^{-}(s_{0}+s_{1})+(\mathfrak{a}-n^{-\omega/2})(s_{0}+s_{1})\leq\breve{v}_{j}\leq\breve{w}_{j}^{+}(s_{0}+s_{1})+(\mathfrak{a}+n^{-\omega/2})(s_{0}+s_{1});\\
&\breve{f}^{-}(s)+(\mathfrak{a}-n^{-\omega/2})s\leq\breve{f}(s)\leq\breve{f}^{+}(s)+(\mathfrak{a}+n^{-\omega/2})s,\\
&\breve{g}^{-}(s)+(\mathfrak{a}-n^{-\omega/2})s\leq\breve{g}(s)\leq\breve{g}^{+}(s)+(\mathfrak{a}+n^{-\omega/2})s,\end{aligned}

for each integer
j\in\big\llbracket\frac{n^{\prime}}{2}-n^{\prime\prime},\frac{n^{\prime}}{2}+n^{\prime\prime}\big\rrbracket
and real number
s\in[0,s_{0}+s_{1}]
.

Let us quickly establish the proposition assuming (
8.15
). To that end, denote the
(2n^{\prime\prime}+1)
-tuples
\mathring{\bm{u}}=(\breve{u}_{n^{\prime}/2-n^{\prime\prime}},\ldots,\breve{u}_{n^{\prime}/2+n^{\prime\prime}})\in\overline{\mathbb{W}}_{2n^{\prime\prime}+1}
and
\mathring{\bm{v}}=(\breve{v}_{n^{\prime}/2-n^{\prime\prime}},\ldots,\breve{v}_{n^{\prime}/2+n^{\prime\prime}})\in\overline{\mathbb{W}}_{2n^{\prime\prime}+1}
. Similarly, for each index
\pm\in\{+,-\}
and real number
s\in[0,s_{0}+s_{1}]
, denote

\displaystyle\mathring{\bm{w}}^{\pm}(s)=\Big(\breve{w}_{n^{\prime}/2-n^{\prime\prime}}^{\pm}(s)+(\mathfrak{a}\pm n^{-\omega/2})s,\ldots,\breve{w}_{n^{\prime}/2+n^{\prime\prime}}^{\pm}(s)+(\mathfrak{a}\pm n^{-\omega/2})s\Big)\in\overline{\mathbb{W}}_{2n^{\prime\prime}+1};

\displaystyle\mathring{f}^{\pm}(s)=\breve{f}^{\pm}(s)+(\mathfrak{a}\pm n^{-\omega/2})s;\qquad\mathring{g}^{\pm}(s)=\breve{g}^{\pm}(s)+(\mathfrak{a}\pm n^{-\omega/2})s.

Then, by the second part of
2.15
and
2.1
, the laws of the processes
\big(\breve{x}_{j}(s)\big)
and
\big(\mathring{w}_{j}^{\pm}(s)\big)
for each
\pm\in\{+,-\}
and
(j,s)\in\big\llbracket\frac{n^{\prime}}{2}-n^{\prime\prime},\frac{n^{\prime}}{2}+n^{\prime\prime}\big\rrbracket\times[0,s_{0}+s_{1}]
are given by
\mathfrak{Q}_{\breve{f};\breve{g}}^{\mathring{\bm{u}};\mathring{\bm{v}}}(n^{-1})
and
\mathfrak{Q}_{\mathring{f}^{\pm};\mathring{g}^{\pm}}^{\mathring{\bm{w}}^{\pm}(0);\mathring{\bm{w}}^{\pm}(s_{0}+s_{1})}(n^{-1})
, respectively. This, together with (
8.15
) and
2.3
yields a coupling on
\mathscr{A}_{1}
that
\breve{\bm{w}}^{-}(s)+(\mathfrak{a}-n^{-\omega/2})s=\mathring{\bm{w}}^{-}(s)\leq\breve{\bm{x}}(s)\leq\mathring{\bm{w}}^{+}(s)=\breve{\bm{w}}^{+}(s)+(\mathfrak{a}+n^{-\omega/2})s
for each
s\in[s_{0},s_{1}]
, verifying the proposition.

The second bound in the first statement of (
8.15
) follows the fact that
\breve{\bm{w}}^{\pm}(0)=\breve{\bm{u}}^{\pm}
; (
8.4
); the fact that
\mathscr{A}_{1}\subseteq\mathscr{A}_{0}
; and the fact that on
\mathscr{A}_{0}
we have (by (
8.2
)) that
u_{j}\leq G(t_{0}-s_{0},jn^{-1})+n^{\omega/4-1}\leq G^{+}(jn^{-1})=\breve{u}_{j}^{+}
for
j\notin\big\llbracket\frac{n^{\prime}}{2}-\frac{2n^{\prime\prime}}{3},\frac{n^{\prime}}{2}+\frac{2n^{\prime\prime}}{3}\big\rrbracket
. The proof of the first bound there is entirely analogous.

For the second statement of (
8.15
), we only show that
\breve{w}_{j}^{-}(s_{0}+s_{1})+(\mathfrak{a}-n^{-\omega/2})(s_{0}+s_{1})\leq\breve{v}_{j}
, as the proof that
\breve{v}_{j}\leq\breve{w}_{j}^{+}(s_{0}+s_{1})+(\mathfrak{a}+n^{-\omega/2})(s_{0}+s_{1})
is entirely analogous. Since we have restricted to the event
\mathscr{A}_{0}
(and since
n=(n^{\prime})^{2}
), we have that

\displaystyle\frac{n}{n^{\prime}}\cdot\breve{v}_{j}\geq\frac{n}{n^{\prime}}\cdot\bigg(G\Big(t_{0}+s_{1},y_{0}-\frac{n^{\prime}}{2n}+\frac{j}{n}\Big)-n^{\omega/4-1}\bigg)=\widetilde{G}\Big(\widetilde{s}_{0}+\widetilde{s}_{1},\displaystyle\frac{j}{n^{\prime}}\Big)-(n^{\prime})^{\omega/2-1},

and so it suffices to show that

\displaystyle\widetilde{w}_{j}^{-}(\widetilde{s}_{0}+\widetilde{s}_{1})\leq\widetilde{G}\Big(\widetilde{s}_{0}+\widetilde{s}_{1},\displaystyle\frac{j}{n^{\prime}}\Big)-(\mathfrak{a}-n^{-\omega/2})(\widetilde{s}_{0}+\widetilde{s}_{1})-(n^{\prime})^{\omega-1}.

Thus, since we have restricted to the event
\mathscr{A}_{1}
from (
8.14
); since for
j\in\big\llbracket\frac{n^{\prime}}{2}-n^{\prime\prime},\frac{n^{\prime}}{2}+n^{\prime\prime}\big\rrbracket
we have

\displaystyle\widetilde{w}_{j}^{-}(0)=\frac{n}{n^{\prime}}\cdot\breve{u}_{j}^{-}\leq\widetilde{G}\Big(0,\frac{j}{n^{\prime}}\Big)+(n^{\prime})^{2\omega/3-1},

by (
8.4
), (
8.12
), and the definition of
G^{-}
; and since
\widetilde{s}_{0}=(n^{\prime})^{\omega-1}
(by (
8.1
) and (
8.11
)), it suffices to show that

\displaystyle\widetilde{G}\Big(0,\frac{j}{n^{\prime}}\Big)\leq\widetilde{G}\Big(\widetilde{s}_{0}+\widetilde{s}_{1},\displaystyle\frac{j}{n^{\prime}}\Big)-\mathfrak{a}(\widetilde{s}_{0}+\widetilde{s}_{1})+(n^{\prime})^{-\omega}\widetilde{s}_{1}-2(n^{\prime})^{\omega-1}.

This follows from the fact that

\displaystyle\bigg|\widetilde{G}\Big(0,\displaystyle\frac{j}{n^{\prime}}\Big)-\widetilde{G}\Big(\widetilde{s}_{0}+\widetilde{s}_{1},\displaystyle\frac{j}{n^{\prime}}\Big)-\mathfrak{a}(\widetilde{s}_{0}+\widetilde{s}_{1})\bigg|\leq B_{0}\Big(\widetilde{s}_{0}+\widetilde{s}_{1}+\displaystyle\frac{n^{\prime\prime}}{n^{\prime}}\Big)^{2}

\displaystyle\leq 9B_{0}\big((n^{\prime})^{12\omega-2}+(n^{\prime})^{-1}\big)

\displaystyle\leq(n^{\prime})^{-\omega}\widetilde{s}_{1}-2(n^{\prime})^{\omega-1},

for sufficiently large
n
. Here, in the first inequality we applied a Taylor expansion, the fact that
\mathfrak{a}=\partial_{t}G(t_{0},y_{0})
, the fact that
\big|j-\frac{n^{\prime}}{2}\big|\leq n^{\prime\prime}
, and the fact that
\|G\|_{\mathcal{C}^{2}(\mathfrak{R})}\leq B_{0}
; in the second inequality, we used the facts that
\widetilde{s}_{0}+\widetilde{s}_{1}\leq 2\widetilde{s}_{1}\leq 2(n^{\prime})^{6\omega-1}
and that
n^{\prime\prime}=(n^{\prime})^{1/2}
; and in the third inequality we used the facts that
\omega\leq\frac{1}{20}
(and again that
\widetilde{s}_{1}=(n^{\prime})^{6\omega-1}
).

It therefore remains to establish the last two statements of (
8.15
); among these, we only verify the bound
\breve{f}^{-}\leq\breve{f}
there, as the proofs of the remaining three are entirely analogous. To that end, observe on
\mathscr{A}_{1}
that for each
s\in[0,s_{0}+s_{1}]
we have, denoting
\widetilde{s}=\frac{ns}{n^{\prime}}
,

(8.16)

\displaystyle\begin{aligned} \frac{n}{n^{\prime}}\cdot\mathring{f}^{-}(s)=\widetilde{w}_{n^{\prime}/2+n^{\prime\prime}+1}^{-}(\widetilde{s})+(\mathfrak{a}-n^{-\omega})\widetilde{s}&\leq\widetilde{w}_{n^{\prime}/2+n^{\prime\prime}+1}^{-}(0)+\mathfrak{a}\widetilde{s}+(n^{\prime})^{2\omega/3-1}\\
&=\widetilde{G}^{-}\Big(\displaystyle\frac{1}{2}+\displaystyle\frac{n^{\prime\prime}+1}{n^{\prime}}\Big)+\mathfrak{a}\widetilde{s}+(n^{\prime})^{2\omega/3-1}\\
&=\widetilde{G}\Big(0,\displaystyle\frac{1}{2}+\displaystyle\frac{n^{\prime\prime}+1}{n^{\prime}}\Big)+\mathfrak{a}\widetilde{s}-(n^{\prime})^{\omega-1}+(n^{\prime})^{2\omega/3-1},\end{aligned}

where in the first statement we applied the definitions of
\mathring{f}
and
\breve{f}^{-}
(as well as (
8.11
)); in the second we used the definition of
\mathscr{A}_{1}
from (
8.14
); in the third we used the fact that
\breve{w}_{n^{\prime}/2+n^{\prime\prime}+1}^{-}(0)=G^{-}\big(y_{0}+\frac{n^{\prime\prime}+1}{n}\big)
(by (
8.4
)) and the rescaling (
8.11
); and in the fourth we used the definition of
\widetilde{G}^{-}
in terms of
\widetilde{G}
. We further have that

\displaystyle\widetilde{G}\Big(

\displaystyle 0,\displaystyle\frac{1}{2}+\displaystyle\frac{n^{\prime\prime}+1}{n^{\prime}}\Big)+\mathfrak{a}\widetilde{s}-(n^{\prime})^{\omega-1}+(n^{\prime})^{2\omega/3-1}

\displaystyle\leq\widetilde{G}\Big(\widetilde{s},\displaystyle\frac{1}{2}+\displaystyle\frac{n^{\prime\prime}+1}{n^{\prime}}\Big)-(n^{\prime})^{3\omega/4-1}+\displaystyle\frac{B_{0}n^{\prime}}{n}\Big(\widetilde{s}+\displaystyle\frac{n^{\prime\prime}+1}{n^{\prime}}\Big)^{2}

\displaystyle\leq\widetilde{x}_{n^{\prime}/2+n^{\prime\prime}+1}(\widetilde{s})-(n^{\prime})^{3\omega/4-1}+\displaystyle\frac{2B_{0}\widetilde{s}^{2}n^{\prime}}{n}+2(n^{\prime})^{\omega/2-1}\leq\displaystyle\frac{n}{n^{\prime}}\cdot\breve{x}_{n^{\prime}/2+n^{\prime\prime}+1}(s)=\displaystyle\frac{n}{n^{\prime}}\cdot\breve{f}(s),

where in the first statement we applied a Taylor expansion for
\widetilde{G}
, using the facts that
\mathfrak{a}=\partial_{t}G(t_{0},y_{0})=\partial_{t}G^{-}(t_{0},y_{0})=\partial_{t}\widetilde{G}^{-}\big(0,\frac{1}{2}\big)
and that
[\widetilde{G}^{-}]_{2}=\frac{n^{\prime}}{n}\cdot[G^{-}]_{2}\leq\frac{2B_{0}n^{\prime}}{n}
; in the second we used the fact that we are restricting to the event
\mathscr{A}_{0}
, (
8.12
), and the fact that
n^{\prime}n^{-1}(n^{\prime\prime}n^{\prime-1})^{2}=n^{-1}
; in the third we used (
8.11
) and the fact that
\widetilde{s}^{2}\leq 4\widetilde{s}_{1}^{2}=4(n^{\prime})^{12\omega-2}\leq(n^{\prime})^{-1}
(as
\omega\leq\frac{1}{20}
); and in the fourth we used the definition of
\breve{f}
. Combining this with (
8.16
) yields the first inequality in the third statement of (
8.15
). As mentioned previously, the proof of the second inequality is entirely analogous, as is the proof of the fourth statement of (
8.15
); this establishes the proposition.
∎

8.3.
Proof of
8.5

To establish
8.5
, we set some additional notation, which will eventually more precisely prescribe the limiting behaviors of the paths in
\widetilde{\bm{w}}(s)
. To that end, let
\widetilde{H}:[0,\widetilde{s}_{0}+\widetilde{s}_{1}]\times\mathbb{R}\rightarrow[0,1]
denote the height function associated with the inverted height function
\widetilde{G}
(recall (
3.4
)), given by
\widetilde{H}(t,x)=\sup\big\{y\in\mathbb{R}:\widetilde{G}(t,y)\geq x\big\}
. In particular, for any
(t,x)\in[0,\widetilde{s}_{0}+\widetilde{s}_{1}]\times[-n^{\omega},n^{\omega}]
, we have

(8.17)

\displaystyle\widetilde{H}(t,x)\quad\text{is the unique value of $y\in\big[-(n^{\prime})^{3\omega/2},(n^{\prime})^{3\omega/2}\big]$ such that $\widetilde{G}(t,y)=x$},

where this uniqueness follows from the fact that
\widetilde{G}(s,y)
is decreasing in
y
(by (
8.13
)), and we implicitly used the fact that any
(s,y)\in[0,\widetilde{s}_{0}+\widetilde{s}_{1}]\times\big[-(n^{\prime})^{3\omega/2},(n^{\prime})^{3\omega/2}\big]
is in the domain of
\widetilde{G}
since
